Episode 39 · Fact-check log

Fact-check log — Viral Election-Fraud Videos: What Did They Really Show?

Independent verification pass, run 2026-10-12 (web-sourced, not trusting the draft). Each claim gets a verdict: CONFIRMED, CORRECTED, UNRESOLVED or APPROXIMATE. This is a REQUIRED gate, set out under "Verification gate" in the episodes README. The show's differentiator is that this file is real.

  • CONFIRMED — the source says what the episode says.
  • CORRECTED — the source says otherwise; the script was changed, and the change is listed under "Corrections applied to source" below. These stay in the file: they are the point of it.
  • UNRESOLVED — could not be established from a source worth trusting, so it is not used on air.
  • APPROXIMATE — checked, roughly right, and deliberately not cited on air. Not the same as UNRESOLVED: the claim was established, the figure just isn't precise enough to state. Use it when the direction is sound but a number would imply more precision than the source supports.

⛔ These four are the whole vocabulary. The site builder reads them to count the claims the public site prints, and since package HFIX a verdict it does not recognise fails the build instead of being skipped silently. A fifth verdict means changing both this file and the builder.

Verification statusadversarial-pass — a peg hunt (2026-10-12: a blind sweep with the topic's own

vocabulary banned, the Federal Register's presidential-documents index for 2026-09-21 to 10-12, and Votebeat's front page as a second index), the deep-research-v3 engine in explainer mode, and a three-way hand pass on the primary record (2026-10-12): (A) Georgia, Fulton County, the 2026 federal seizure and the machine record; (B) every other viral clip and story, from 2020, 2022 and 2024 and from both sides, plus the proven cases in Bridgeport and Paterson; (C) voter rolls, noncitizen audits, the national scale of proven fraud, North Carolina's 9th District, the 2020 close-out record and the research on how a viewer can check a clip. The hand passes opened the court orders, sworn declarations, board minutes and transcripts, audit reports, statutes and inspector-general reports themselves wherever they could be fetched; where a page was blocked, it was read through an archived copy and that is said beside it. Every quotation relied on below was checked mechanically against the saved source text. Where a source names a private person (an election worker, a poll watcher, a voter, a defendant, a relative), the quote is cut before the name, or the name is replaced by a role in square brackets.

Top-up (2026-10-13): after the pitch, five further hand passes answered the host's questions on proven fraud and its coverage, the states that refused the federal demand for their voter rolls, "statistically impossible" counts, Pennsylvania's undated mail ballots, and noncitizen and other ineligible voting, and brought the peg up to date. They are § 4 (entries 213–379), held to the same standard: primaries opened wherever they could be fetched, both directions tested, every quotation checked mechanically. Where a top-up corrected an earlier entry, that entry now points to the new one.

§ 1. Why now — what was hunted, and what was rejected

The hunt ran on 2026-10-12 with the topic's own words banned from the first round of queries, so that it would find what is live rather than what is famous. The Federal Register returned 17 presidential documents for 2026-09-21 to 10-12, none of them on this subject. Votebeat's front page was the index that found the events.

  • Chosen, for the script (structural): the FBI still holds Fulton County's 2020 ballots. Agents seized them in January 2026 under a search warrant, and in May 2026 a federal judge refused the county's request to have them returned. Fulton is the county where the most-shared 2020 clip was filmed, and the unsealed affidavit is itself a case file: it says some allegations were disproven and some substantiated. That is exactly the episode's distinction between real sloppiness and fraud.
  • Chosen, for the description (the hard date): Georgia's advance voting opens 2026-10-13. The State Election Board's executive director sent letters dated 2026-09-30 asking the Justice Department and Homeland Security to intervene over the state's voting system, and the county election officials' association asked the board on 2026-10-08 to rescind them.
  • Rejected: a count of fewer 2020-denying candidates on 2026 ballots (285 to 197): it is about candidates, not the evidence, and the show does not make candidates the subject. Candidate deepfakes (March 2026): seven months old, and episode 27's ground. Mail delays (2026-10-12) and a county ballot misprint (2026-10-02): process glitches, not viral evidence, and episodes 30 and 37's ground.
  • Kept as a case-file fact, not the peg: the Justice Department's 2025–26 lawsuits for states' full voter rolls (most dismissed), which belong in the "bad voter rolls" case.
  • The peg hunt carried two figures from the press that the hand pass then corrected against the primary record: the seizure date and the number of boxes (entries 35 and 36).
  • Peg update, 2026-10-13 (entries 231–235 and 268):
  • Fulton: still no charge. In April 2026 a federal grand jury subpoenaed the details of everyone who worked the 2020 count; a district judge quashed that subpoena on July 7, 2026; the Justice Department appealed on Aug 6 (Eleventh Circuit No. 26-12769) and filed its opening brief on Oct 5, arguing that some 2020 image files "may have been altered in 2024". The ballots remain with the FBI.
  • The board director's Sept 30 letters have not been rescinded. The Justice Department said only that it was aware of them; three of the five board members say they never approved the last request; no board meeting is posted before Dec 16.
  • On Oct 9, 2026 a federal judge in Washington, D.C. vacated the Justice Department's nationwide program of collecting states' full voter rolls. No appeal or stay request was found as of Oct 13.
  • Homeland Security's "more than 42,000 deceased registrants" figure still has no published method; the letter that used it was addressed to the Attorney General, not to Georgia (entry 262).

§ 2. The engine's own work

deep-research-v3, explainer mode, run wf_3799ea2e-aa7 (stamped 2026-10-13): 6 angles of 4 sub-questions, 41 agents in all (24 finders, 12 primary-source verifications, 3 completeness critics, plus scoping and synthesis), 4,173,733 subagent tokens. It found 120 unique claims. Twelve were verified with the primary opened, and all twelve held; 108 were reported but not adjudicated by the engine; its critics named 18 gaps. The hand pass below re-read the engine's findings against the primaries and changed several that would have reached the script: the seizure date and the box count; the claim that the Republican watchers' affidavits said no one was told to leave; the time window for the 9,752 extra ballots; the call that the "same stack scanned over and over" clip was pure misreading (about 200 ballots really were double-scanned in the first count); the "unsettled" dead-voter count (four, under oath); the size and meaning of the 3,075 repeated recount images; and the denominator in the Antrim hand count. The critics' gaps also added cases the engine had missed: Bridgeport 2023, Paterson 2020, the voting-machine vulnerability record, and the noncitizen audits.

§ 3. Hand pass, by case file

Each case file carries a tray on its first line, under the host's ruling of 2026-10-13: EXPLAINED (the record breaks the claim as shared), PROVEN (real fraud or real error, with its scale), OPEN (only when a specific gap in the official record, the evidence that would settle it, and who holds that evidence can all be named), or NOT CHECKABLE (for example, fraud that leaves no record). A tray is a call on the case; the four verdict words stay on the individual claims. On screen, EXPLAINED and PROVEN are shown as SOLID (settled) and OPEN as GLASS (still argued).

Case file: State Farm Arena — "suitcases of ballots pulled from under a table" (Georgia, Nov 3, 2020)

Tray: EXPLAINED. Sealed carriers of opened, uncounted ballots, stored in view and brought back out when counting resumed; three agencies reviewed the full tape. The social-media confession was fabricated.

1. The containers held already-opened, not-yet-counted absentee ballots, sealed and stored under the table about 10 p.m. in front of monitors and media

CONFIRMED. The Secretary of State's chief investigator said so in a sworn declaration of Dec 5, 2020.

  • "Around 10 p.m., with the room full of people, including official monitors and the media, video shows ballots that had already been opened but not counted placed in the boxes, sealed up, stored under the table."
  • "This was done because employees thought that they were done for the night and were closing up and ready to leave."
  • https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.284055/gov.uscourts.gand.284055.72.1.pdf (a scan; quotes checked against an OCR of it)

2. The timeline: box under the table at 10:29 p.m. with the media present; media and observers leave at 10:46; boxes come out and scanning resumes at 11:03

CONFIRMED. The State Election Board's report of investigation (case SEB2020-059, dated March 7, 2023) sets out the minute-by-minute timeline from the footage.

  • "is observed placing ballot box underneath a table with the media present." (the 10:29 p.m. entry; the worker's name is removed)
  • "At 10:46 p.m., with Fulton County elections employees still in the room, the media and observers leave the room."
  • "At 11:03 p.m., Fulton County elections employees start unpacking, retrieving ballot boxes from underneath tables, and transporting ballots to scanners for scanning."
  • https://sos.ga.gov/sites/default/files/2023-06/SEB2020-059%20ROI%20redacted.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

3. The table came in empty that morning, and no ballots were brought in from outside

CONFIRMED. Same sworn declaration: the table was brought in at 8:22 a.m.

  • "there were no mystery ballots that were brought in from an unknown location and hidden under tables"
  • https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.284055/gov.uscourts.gand.284055.72.1.pdf

4. FBI, GBI and Secretary of State investigators reviewed the entire unedited footage and found no fraud

CONFIRMED. The board's report of investigation itself carries the words; they do not come only from a press release.

  • "Investigators from the three law enforcement agencies also reviewed the entire unedited security video footage of the events in question at State Farm Arena."
  • "There was no evidence of any type of fraud as alleged."
  • "The investigation did not uncover any violations."
  • https://sos.ga.gov/sites/default/files/2023-06/SEB2020-059%20ROI%20redacted.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

5. The State Election Board dismissed the "poll worker fraud" case on June 20, 2023

CONFIRMED. The board's meeting summary lists it among the dismissed cases.

  • "*The Board voted to dismiss the following cases:" … "Case No. 2020-059 Fulton County (Poll Worker Fraud)"
  • https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/20230630__summary.pdf

6. The containers were ballot carriers sealed with tamper-proof seals, not suitcases

CONFIRMED. From sworn and official descriptions (congressional hearing, June 21, 2022). The footage itself was not frame-checked by this pass, so the episode describes the containers only in these words.

  • "putting ballots that are prepared to be scanned into ballot carriers that are then sealed with tamper-proof seals" (the Secretary of State's chief operating officer, under oath)
  • "you can see the election monitors in the corner with the press as they are taking these ballot carriers and putting them under the table."
  • "There is no suitcase. There is a wheeled bin where they carry the ballots." (the then-Acting Deputy Attorney General, recounting what he told the then-President)
  • "They actually reviewed approximately 48 hours" (of footage)
  • https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CHRG-117hhrg49352/html/CHRG-117hhrg49352.htm

7. The "water leak" was an overflowing urinal that morning and did not affect the evening count

CONFIRMED.

  • "the incident initially reported as a water leak late in the evening on November 3rd was actually a urinal that had overflowed early in the morning of November 3rd, and did not affect the counting of votes by Fulton County later that evening."
  • https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.284055/gov.uscourts.gand.284055.72.1.pdf

8. The social-media "confession" attributed to one of the workers was fake

CONFIRMED. The state's investigators obtained a search warrant for the account and the FBI interviewed the person who made it. The original complaint to the state had been built on these posts: a fabricated item inside the same story.

  • "The account creator admitted he created the fake account and confirmed the content that was posted on the account was fake."
  • https://sos.ga.gov/sites/default/files/2023-06/SEB2020-059%20ROI%20redacted.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

9. A court later found the tapes show no illegal activity, and a state official had called it "normal ballot processing" on Dec 4, 2020

CONFIRMED. New York's Appellate Division, First Department (July 2, 2024), in a disciplinary ruling.

  • "SFA tapes simply do not show any illegal activity" (the disciplinary referee's finding, as recited by the court)
  • "normal ballot processing had occurred."
  • https://www.nycourts.gov/reporter/3dseries/2024/2024_03561.htm

10. The former President's personal lawyer, who spread the clip, was disbarred in New York on July 2, 2024

CONFIRMED. A ruling about a speaker's conduct, not part of the evidence base.

  • "is disbarred from the practice of law, effective immediately"
  • https://www.nycourts.gov/reporter/3dseries/2024/2024_03561.htm

11. The two election workers shown in the clip won "about $148 million" in defamation damages

CORRECTED. The jury verdict (Dec 15, 2023) was $148,169,000, of which $75 million was punitive. The final judgment entered on the parties' joint stipulation (Dec 18, 2023) was $145,969,000 in damages, plus fee awards; the judgment does not say why it is lower. Liability was entered by default, as a sanction for discovery failures, so the jury decided only damages. The judgment nonetheless declares the statements false. The post-trial motion was denied on Apr 15, 2024 as a final, appealable order (D.D.C. No. 1:21-cv-03354).

  • "damages in the amount of $145,969,000.00"
  • "It is hereby DECLARED (1) that the Actionable Statements set forth in the Amended Complaint, ECF No. 22, are false"
  • "This is a final and appealable order."
  • https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.dcd.238720/gov.uscourts.dcd.238720.142.0.pdf (judgment) · https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.dcd.238720/gov.uscourts.dcd.238720.135.0.pdf (verdict form, read by hand from the scan) · https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/USCOURTS-dcd-1_21-cv-03354/pdf/USCOURTS-dcd-1_21-cv-03354-3.pdf (Apr 15, 2024 order)

Case file: the observers and the state monitor at State Farm Arena

Tray: PROVEN as a process failure (a stop was announced, observers left believing counting was over, counting resumed without them, and the state monitor was out from about 8:15 to 11:52 p.m.). EXPLAINED as an order to leave or secret counting. NOT CHECKABLE: whether anyone was told to leave (the accounts conflict; there is no audio).

12. "Both Republican watchers' affidavits confirmed no one was told to leave"

CORRECTED. That is wrong as worded. The watchers' affidavits (Nov 2020) alleged that a woman yelled for work to stop and resume the next morning, and one affidavit later says the watcher was told to leave. In their interviews with a state investigator (Jan 12, 2021), both said no one told them to leave, but that a county employee announced the room was stopping work. What the record supports: a stop for the night was announced to the room, the observers left on their own believing counting was over, no one ordered them out, and counting resumed after a call from the county elections director. The fault is in the process (no one told the observers), not fraud by the workers.

  • "Both poll watchers confirmed that no one was told or instructed to leave State Farm Arena." (the report's findings)
  • One watcher in interview: "we are calling it, let" […]
  • The other: a county employee "advised everyone in the entire room they were going to stop working for the night"
  • "However, in the latter part of his affidavit he states he was told to leave." (the investigator's note)
  • "discovered that observers and media were not asked to leave. They simply left on their own when they saw one group of workers, whose job was only to open envelopes and who had completed that task, also leave." (the state's sworn account)
  • https://sos.ga.gov/sites/default/files/2023-06/SEB2020-059%20ROI%20redacted.pdf (read via the Internet Archive) · https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.284055/gov.uscourts.gand.284055.72.1.pdf

13. The State Election Board's monitor was out of the room from about 8:15 p.m. to 11:52 p.m.

APPROXIMATE. This rests on a fact-checker's interview with the unnamed monitor (FactCheck.org, Dec 2020); his own written account was not found. On air: about 8:15 to about midnight, attributed.

  • "told us he was in the room until about 8:15 p.m., when he left, and returned at 11:52 p.m."
  • The deputy secretary of state "told us the monitor was not required to be there at all times, but she instructed him to return after hearing from news outlets that the county was going to cease counting for the night."
  • https://www.factcheck.org/2020/12/video-doesnt-show-suitcases-of-illegal-ballots-in-georgia/

14. The 9,752 extra ballots were scanned while the monitor was out (8:15 to 11:52 p.m.)

CORRECTED. The source's count spans 8:15 p.m. to the 12:43 a.m. stop, not the monitor's absence. For comparison, an attorney had claimed 18,000 ballots could have been run after observers left; the count for the whole four and a half hours is about half of that.

  • "Between 8:15 p.m. and 12:43 a.m., the change in total ballots went from 89,381 ballots to 99,133 ballots"
  • https://www.factcheck.org/2020/12/video-doesnt-show-suitcases-of-illegal-ballots-in-georgia/

15. The decision to keep counting came from above, around 10:30 p.m.

CONFIRMED.

  • The county elections director "advised in an email that, sometime around 10:30 p.m. on election night, he called" the elections chief "to inquire if it were true that they were shutting down for the night."
  • "we were told that it looked like they were shutting down the Fulton County counting. The secretary expressed some displeasure at that because we wanted everybody to keep counting" (the Secretary of State's chief operating officer, under oath)
  • https://sos.ga.gov/sites/default/files/2023-06/SEB2020-059%20ROI%20redacted.pdf (read via the Internet Archive) · https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CHRG-117hhrg49352/html/CHRG-117hhrg49352.htm

16. No rule required counting to pause when the watchers left

UNRESOLVED. The state board rule and the statutes in force that night were not opened. The report records one watcher's understanding of when watchers may be present, which is not the rule. On air, at most: we found no rule that required them to stop.

  • "poll watchers can be present from when the lights are turned on and until the doors are locked" (a watcher's understanding, as recorded)
  • https://sos.ga.gov/sites/default/files/2023-06/SEB2020-059%20ROI%20redacted.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

Case file: the rescan clip — "the same stack of ballots scanned over and over" (Fulton, 2020)

Tray: EXPLAINED as fraud (the clip shows a standard rescan). PROVEN as sloppiness: about 200 ballots double-scanned in the first count, and 3,075 repeated images in the recount.

17. Running a batch through again after a mis-scan is standard procedure

CONFIRMED.

  • "What happens there is a standard operating procedure if there is a mis-scan, if there is a misalignment, if it doesn't read right." … "After a mis-scan, you have to delete that batch and put it back through again." (the Secretary of State's chief operating officer, under oath)
  • On double-scanning, the county elections chief told investigators "it is possible to occur; however, it will cause an error."
  • https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/CHRG-117hhrg49352/html/CHRG-117hhrg49352.htm · https://sos.ga.gov/sites/default/files/2023-06/SEB2020-059%20ROI%20redacted.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

18. In Georgia's voting system, rejecting a batch deletes its tallied votes but keeps its images, so duplicate images can exist without duplicate votes

CONFIRMED. Both hand passes found this in Georgia's 2024 ballot-image audit report (written by the state's audit contractor and read from a Princeton-hosted copy), and the state's investigator said the same to the board on May 7, 2024. It explains how the system handles a rejected batch; it does not prove what happened in any one frame of the clip.

  • "rejecting a batch of ballots in the Dominion voting system would remove the tabulated results for that batch, but not the ballot images."
  • "This is also why rejected images might appear in county exports provided in response to open records requests."
  • "only the batch that is published and validated is" […] "included in the results. As a result of this, there are instances where a county may have" […] "repeated images without the repeated images being" […] "included in the tabulated results." (the state investigator; the brackets mark transcript line numbers)
  • https://blog.citp.princeton.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/952/2026/04/georgia_ballot_image_audit_report-_november_general_0.pdf · https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/2024-05-07__transcript.pdf

19. The rescan clip is pure misreading: nothing was double-counted

CORRECTED. The clip shows a standard rescan, but about 200 ballots in two batches really were double-scanned in Fulton's initial 2020 count, as the state's investigators substantiated. Whether those are the batch in the viral clip is not established, so they are not tied to that frame.

  • "Secretary Investigators substantiated the allegations that two batches totaling almost 200 ballots were double scanned during the initial count of the November 2020 election."
  • "something that should never happen."
  • Causes the board names: "poor batch management and storage practices", the time crunch, and "a corresponding increase in paper jams".
  • https://sos.ga.gov/sites/default/files/forms/Performance%20Review%20Board%20Report%20on%20Fulton%20County%20Elections%20%281-13-23%29_0%20%28OLD%29.pdf (Performance Review Board report, Jan 13, 2023; read via the Internet Archive)

20. A fact-checker's "0.153%" hand-count gap is a typo for the state's 0.1053%

CONFIRMED. The state's audit memo gives 0.1053% (entry 22).

  • https://sos.ga.gov/admin/uploads/11.19_.20_Risk_Limiting_Audit_Report_Memo_1.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

Case file: what the paper said — Georgia's 2020 hand count

Tray: EXPLAINED for the claim that the machines or the count were wrong: the paper hand count matched within 0.1053%. PROVEN for the forgotten memory cards found by the audit (small, and corrected).

21. The hand count covered about 5.0 million ballots in 41,881 batches across all 159 counties

CONFIRMED. The audit's totals row gives 5,000,585 ballots by hand against 4,995,323 by machine, with the same winner and a margin that moved by 496 votes.

  • "Audit boards from all 159 Georgia counties examined 41881 batches"
  • https://sos.ga.gov/admin/uploads/11.19_.20_Risk_Limiting_Audit_Report_Memo_1.pdf · https://sos.ga.gov/admin/uploads/county-summary-data.pdf (both read via the Internet Archive)

22. The gap was 0.1053% of votes and 0.0099% of the margin, against an expected 1.0–1.5% from human counting error

CONFIRMED. 0.1053% is about one ballot in 950.

  • "Georgia shows a 0.1053% (0.001053) variation in statewide total vote count, and a 0.0099% (0.000099) variation in the overall margin."
  • "ranges anywhere from 1.0 - 1.5% across all ballot types."
  • https://sos.ga.gov/admin/uploads/11.19_.20_Risk_Limiting_Audit_Report_Memo_1.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

23. A risk-limiting audit confirms the winner, not the exact margin

CONFIRMED.

  • "Like any risk-limiting audit, this audit does not confirm or correct the exact margin of victory."
  • https://sos.ga.gov/admin/uploads/11.19_.20_Risk_Limiting_Audit_Report_Memo_1.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

24. Fulton: the hand count found 525,293 ballots (634 more than first reported), a 243,559-vote margin, and a net shift of 345 votes toward the losing candidate

CONFIRMED. From the county summary table's Fulton row. 345 on a 243,559-vote margin is about 1 vote in 700 (our arithmetic).

  • https://sos.ga.gov/admin/uploads/county-summary-data.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

25. No county's margin moved more than 0.73%, and 103 of 159 moved less than 0.05%

CONFIRMED.

  • "no individual county showed a variation in margin larger than 0.73%, and 103 of the 159 counties showed a margin variation of less than 0.05%."
  • https://sos.ga.gov/admin/uploads/11.19_.20_Risk_Limiting_Audit_Report_Memo_1.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

26. Forgotten memory cards in four counties (Fayette, Floyd, Walton, Douglas) netted about 1,200 votes toward the losing candidate

APPROXIMATE. The state's press release confirms the type of error, and the audit table confirms Floyd County's 2,464-ballot correction, but the four names and the 1,200 net come only from secondary sources. On air: a few counties had forgotten to upload memory cards; fixing that helped the losing side.

  • "The audit process also led to counties catching making mistakes they made in their original count by not uploading all memory cards."
  • https://web.archive.org/web/20201121000000/https://sos.ga.gov/index.php/elections/historic_first_statewide_audit_of_paper_ballots_upholds_result_of_presidential_race · https://sos.ga.gov/admin/uploads/county-summary-data.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

27. Georgia recertified on Dec 7, 2020 after the machine recount

CONFIRMED. (the Secretary of State's press release of that date, read via the Internet Archive).

  • "Both recounts upheld the original outcome of the race."

28. The recount margin was 11,779, and Fulton's recount (523,779) was the least complete of its three counts

APPROXIMATE. The 523,779 appears in the state investigator's May 7, 2024 presentation as Fulton's presidential total at the second upload; the 11,779 statewide recount margin was not found in a primary. Say under 12,000 if needed.

  • https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/2024-05-07__transcript.pdf

Case file: Fulton 2020 was badly run — not shown to be rigged

Tray: PROVEN as sloppiness (disorganisation, chain-of-custody lapses, missing images). EXPLAINED as rigged. OPEN — whether Fulton's repeated scans and missing images were accident or intent: (a) the state found the sloppiness but could not say whether any repeated image was tabulated, or why; (b) the FBI's forensic findings on the 653 seized boxes, or any charging document, would settle it; (c) the FBI and the Justice Department (N.D. Ga.) hold them. Even if every repeated image had been counted, the hand-pass arithmetic puts it at a few hundred net votes against a statewide margin of about 12,000.

29. The court-ordered monitor saw no fraud but "disorganization, sloppiness, and mismanagement"

CONFIRMED. Through the primary documents that quote the monitor's report; the report itself sits behind an account sign-up and was not read. The two citing documents date it Jan 12 and Feb 16, 2021; say "early 2021".

  • "Seven Hills Strategies' report noted that it saw no instances of dishonesty, fraud, or intentional malfeasance, but that it did see areas of disorganization, sloppiness, and mismanagement." (the Performance Review Board report)
  • "there were chain of custody issues, ballots left unattended, unsealed bags being used, and auditors not recording seal numbers on ballot bags." (the report, as quoted in the federal order of May 6, 2026)
  • https://sos.ga.gov/sites/default/files/forms/Performance%20Review%20Board%20Report%20on%20Fulton%20County%20Elections%20%281-13-23%29_0%20%28OLD%29.pdf (read via the Internet Archive) · https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.108.0.pdf

30. The state's Performance Review Board (Jan 13, 2023) found no evidence of fraud or of anything that would have changed the result, and pointed to three matching counts

CONFIRMED.

  • "we do not see any evidence of fraud, intentional misconduct, or large systematic issues that would have affected the result of the November 2020 election." (as quoted in the FBI affidavit, para 82)
  • "The fact that three separate counts of the 2020 presidential race in Fulton County (initial count, hand-audit count, and machine recount) all showed very similar results supports this conclusion."
  • https://sos.ga.gov/sites/default/files/forms/Performance%20Review%20Board%20Report%20on%20Fulton%20County%20Elections%20%281-13-23%29_0%20%28OLD%29.pdf (read via the Internet Archive) · https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.107.1.pdf

31. In May 2024 the State Election Board found 3,075 duplicate ballot images in the recount, reprimanded the county 2-1 and required a 2024 monitor

CORRECTED. In the detail. The 3,075 are repeated images: 1,806 of machine-printed ballots and 1,269 of hand-marked ones, and investigators said the 1,806 could not be definitively identified as duplicates, because identical machine-printed ballots look alike. Whether any were counted could not be confirmed. The number comes from the investigator's presentation (the transcript), not the minutes. The 2-1 motion was a letter of reprimand plus a memorandum of understanding on a "mutually agreeable" monitor, not an order imposing one, and motions to refer the case to the Attorney General failed that day. Asked whether there was evidence of duplicating across all three counts, the investigator answered "No, sir."

  • "resulting in the final total of 3, 075 repeated ballot images of which 1806 were images of BMD ballots and 1269 were images of hand—marked paper ballots" (an OCR of the scanned transcript)
  • "Fulton County used improper procedures during the recount of the presidential contest in 2020." … "But what cannot be decided conclusively or confirmed conclusively is whether or not those duplicative ballot images were included in the" (the Secretary of State's general counsel)
  • "to issue a letter of reprimand to Fulton County, and enter a memorandum of understanding between Fulton County, the Office of the Secretary of State, and the State Election Board, outlining a mutually agreeable monitor for the 2024 election cycle by the next hearing." … "the motion passed 2-1."
  • https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/2024-05-07__transcript.pdf · https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/20240507__minutes-1.pdf

32. On Aug 6, 2024 the board voted 3-2 to refer the case to the Attorney General

CONFIRMED.

  • "to refer the case to Attorney General's office with the instruction to immediately investigate with outside investigators"
  • https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/20240806__minutes.pdf

33. The Attorney General's office declined on Aug 19, 2024, saying the board cannot direct it to investigate

APPROXIMATE. The Attorney General's letter was not found, and the board's Aug 19, 2024 minutes do not mention it; the wording rests on secondary reports. Say the state Attorney General's office declined, saying the board can't direct it.

  • https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/20240819__minutes.pdf (checked; silent on it) · https://www.democracydocket.com/news-alerts/georgia-attorney-general-says-state-board-cant-direct-his-office-to-investigate-2020-election/ (secondary)

34. The 2024 monitors, working with the Carter Center, found Fulton's 2024 election "organized and orderly"

CONFIRMED. (final report, March 2025).

  • "Fulton County conducted an organized and orderly election process that enabled voters to cast their votes securely and conveniently and ensured that votes were tabulated accurately."
  • "showed substantial improvement when compared with the 2020 election."

Case file: the 2026 FBI seizure, the affidavit, and the judge's order (the peg)

Tray: EXPLAINED for both the FBI proved fraud and it was all debunked. OPEN — whether Fulton's repeated scans and missing images were accident or intent: (a) the state found the sloppiness but could not say whether any repeated image was tabulated, or why; (b) the FBI's forensic findings on the 653 seized boxes, or any charging document, would settle it; (c) the FBI and the Justice Department (N.D. Ga.) hold them. Even if every repeated image had been counted, the hand-pass arithmetic puts it at a few hundred net votes against a statewide margin of about 12,000. No charges have been reported.

35. The FBI seized Fulton's 2020 ballots on Jan 29, 2026

CORRECTED. The date is January 28, 2026: the warrant was signed at 2:31 p.m. and executed at 3:00 p.m. that day, and the judge's order gives the same date.

  • "Date and time warrant executed: 01/28/2026 3:00 pm" (the warrant return, an OCR of a scan)
  • "On January 28, 2026, after obtaining a search warrant signed by a United States Magistrate Judge" … "the Federal Bureau of Investigation" … "seized over 600 boxes"
  • https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.43.2.pdf · https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.108.0.pdf

36. Agents took 656 boxes

CORRECTED. The FBI's own inventory says 653 (24 pallets plus one box, with four counts corrected at inventory). That fits both the order's "over 600 boxes" and the county's "over 650 boxes"; 656 was a press estimate.

  • "Total boxes seized: 653."
  • https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.43.2.pdf

37. The affidavit says some allegations were disproven and some substantiated, and names "unknown persons"

CONFIRMED. The same affidavit was also filed as Doc. 22-1 in the warrant matter.

  • "Some of those allegations have been disproven while some of those allegations have been substantiated, including through admissions by Fulton County." (para 6)
  • "This warrant application is part of an FBI criminal investigation into whether any of the improprieties were intentional acts that violated federal criminal laws." (para 6)
  • "have been committed by unknown persons." (para 4)
  • https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.107.1.pdf · https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355087/gov.uscourts.gand.355087.22.1.pdf

38. The affidavit lists five real defects: missing images for 528,777 and 527,925 ballots, rescans, inconsistent hand-audit batch tallies, uncreased absentee ballots, and a 511,343 recount figure

CONFIRMED. The county's own reply says duplication may have happened through human error. On the uncreased ballots, the state's 2021 explanation was that duplicated emergency, military, overseas and damaged ballots are not folded.

  • "Fulton County has admitted that it does not have scanned images of all the 528,777 ballots counted during the Original Count or the 527,925 ballots counted during the Recount."
  • "recount totaling 511,343 ballots, 17,434 ballots fewer than original counted."
  • "if it did occur, it happened due to human error." (Fulton County, para 38)
  • "There is absolutely no evidence that this happened a single time in Georgia." (the Secretary of State's letter to Congress, Jan 6, 2021, on uncreased ballots)
  • https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.107.1.pdf · https://sos.ga.gov/sites/default/files/2022-02/Letter_to_Congress_from_Secretary_Raffensperger_%281-6-21%29.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

39. The "40%" figure: the duplicated ballots leaned toward the losing candidate

CONFIRMED. With its base stated. The analyst compared 40% with a 30% county share; in the state's hand count the losing candidate's actual Fulton share was 26.2%. Either way the duplicates leaned somewhat toward the losing candidate, and most still went to the winner. The analyst read it as "not partisan", the state's investigators as not intentional misconduct, and the judge wrote that the duplicates appeared to favour that candidate.

  • "could be intentional but was not partisan" … "(40% instead of 30%)" (the analyst, in the affidavit)
  • "The investigators concluded it was not intentional misconduct." (a former state investigator, in the affidavit)
  • "Indeed, the duplicate ballots appeared to favor" (the judge, fn. 5)
  • https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.107.1.pdf · https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.108.0.pdf · https://sos.ga.gov/admin/uploads/county-summary-data.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

40. The judge called the affidavit "defective in some respects", the 17,434 figure "misleading", and the whole "far from perfect", but said no officer "intentionally lied"

CONFIRMED. (order of May 6, 2026, pp. 36–38).

  • "the Court does recognize that the Affidavit was defective in some respects."
  • "this statement was misleading given that there was a scanner programming error and Fulton County was unable to finish scanning the ballots on time."
  • "While the Affidavit was certainly far from perfect, this is not a situation where an officer left out all the facts that might undermine probable cause or where an officer intentionally lied."
  • "[a]t least 20 times the [A]ffidavit included contrary information and perspectives."
  • It "failed to explain that this modification date could have been caused by personnel from the Secretary of State's Office simply renaming the file"
  • https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.108.0.pdf

41. The order is a narrow ruling on returning property, not a finding about fraud

CONFIRMED. The county's motion under Federal Rule of Criminal Procedure 41(g) was denied on a "callous disregard" standard, with a warning about holding the records too long.

  • "Petitioners' Amended Motion for Return of Property pursuant to Federal Rule of Criminal Procedure 41(g) [Doc. 30] is DENIED."
  • "Respondent has only had the records in its possession for just over three months"
  • "If Respondent retains the records at issue here for a lengthy period, beyond what would be necessary for bringing criminal charges, Petitioners may well be able to establish callous disregard at that juncture." (fn. 37)
  • https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.108.0.pdf

42. The referral came from the presidentially appointed Director of Election Security and Integrity, on Jan 5, 2026

CONFIRMED.

  • "the presidentially appointed Director of Election Security and Integrity."
  • "formally referred the investigation to the FBI."
  • https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.355452/gov.uscourts.gand.355452.108.0.pdf (p. 10)

43. Georgia did not require counties to keep ballot images in 2020

CONFIRMED. The requirement came with Senate Bill 202 in 2021.

  • "Furthermore, the preservation of ballot images was not required in 2020. It was not until the passage of Senate Bill 202 in 2021 that ballots images were deemed public records" (the state investigator, May 7, 2024)
  • https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/2024-05-07__transcript.pdf

44. The state had already examined the 17,434 "shortfall": the complainants conflated ballots with votes, and the late report came from a scanner programming error

CONFIRMED. The OCR of the transcript misreads the figures; the affidavit's 511,343 and 17,434 are used.

  • "conflated ballots cast with total votes" … "to a count discrepancy caused by a scanner programming error"
  • https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/2024-05-07__transcript.pdf

45. The return-of-property case is closed, with no appeal on the docket

CONFIRMED. As of the public docket copy: judgment for the government on May 7, 2026; later entries (to July 8, 2026) concern only the return of exhibits; no notice of appeal.

  • "CLERK'S JUDGMENT in favor of Defendants."
  • https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/72267501/pitts-v-united-states/

46. No one has been charged since the seizure

UNRESOLVED. The order's footnote assumes no charges as of May 6, 2026. This pass found no report of charges through early July 2026 (secondary search results only), and Votebeat on Oct 4, 2026 still describes a criminal investigation. Re-check on the day of the script.

  • Update, 2026-10-13: still no charge; a grand-jury subpoena for the 2020 count workers' details was quashed in July and the government has appealed (entries 231–233).

Case file: the State Election Board's letters, and Georgia's voting-machine and QR-code record

Tray: EXPLAINED for your vote may not count and for QR-code counting being unlawful this November. PROVEN for the real vulnerabilities on the record and the Coffee County breach, with no evidence of exploitation and audits that match the paper.

48. The letters are dated Sept 30, 2026, from the board's executive director to the Attorney General and the Homeland Security Secretary

CONFIRMED. They ask the Justice Department to bar tabulation the director calls non-compliant, set a six-month deadline for a new system, and investigate alleged destruction of records.

  • "I am writing in my capacity as Executive Director of the Georgia State Election Board"
  • "Prohibit non-HAVA-compliant electronic tabulation." · "Set a binding six-month deadline for a HAVA-compliant system." · "Investigate the alleged alteration, destruction, or withholding of ballot images, Cast Vote Records, voter rolls, and related election records"
  • A claim in the letters: "The system can be compromised in seconds with an ordinary ballpoint pen, as demonstrated in federal court."
  • https://www.documentcloud.org/documents/28719569 · https://www.documentcloud.org/documents/28719568

49. A Sept 28 board resolution authorised seeking federal help (reported as a 3-2 vote), and the board's chair says the letters went beyond it

APPROXIMATE. The resolution's text is primary; the 3-2 tally and the chair's position come from reporting, because no minutes of the Sept 28, 2026 meeting had been posted.

  • The executive director "is directed and empowered to immediately seek assistance from any quarter", including "seeking assistance from the DOJ and the DHS"
  • "went well beyond what the board had approved in a 3-2 vote two days earlier" (Votebeat)
  • https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Dominion-Update-RESOLUTION-Coordination-presented-by-Member-Carolyn-Roddy-9.28.26.pdf · https://www.votebeat.org/national/2026/10/04/georgia-election-board-mills-federal-letters-dhs-doj-board-approval/

50. Georgia's QR-code tabulation ban takes effect July 1, 2026 (as the letters say)

CORRECTED. Senate Bill 189 (2024) set July 1, 2026 for the rule that the printed text, not the QR code, is the official count. The legislature then moved that date: SB 3EX, from the 2026 special session and signed June 25, 2026, makes the section effective January 1, 2028. So QR-code tabulation is lawful in Georgia this November. The county officials' association letter says the same.

  • "consistent with O.C.G.A. § 21-2-379.23(d) (effective July 1, 2026)." (the letter to the Justice Department)
  • "Section 7 of this Act shall become effective on July 1, 2026." (SB 189 as enacted)
  • "Section 7 of this Act shall become effective on January 1, 2028." (SB 3EX)
  • "This summer the General Assembly passed SB 3EX, which moved the implementation date to January 1, 2028." (the association's letter)
  • https://www.legis.ga.gov/api/legislation/document/20232024/229578 · https://www.legis.ga.gov/api/legislation/document/2026EX/249745 · https://www.legis.ga.gov/legislation/74767 · https://www.documentcloud.org/documents/28738039

51. The county election officials' association letter asking the board to rescind is dated Oct 8, 2026

CONFIRMED. This settles an Oct 8 vs Oct 9 conflict: Votebeat reported it as sent "on Friday"; the letter is dated the 8th.

  • "Repeated calls telling Georgians their voting system is compromised, days before advance voting, tells them that their vote may not count."
  • "The upgrade simply cannot be completed before November 3"
  • "Every voter reviews a paper ballot that prints their selections in plain text"
  • https://www.documentcloud.org/documents/28738039 · https://www.votebeat.org/national/2026/10/09/georgia-state-election-board-gmail-account-transparency-settlement/

52. Advance voting in Georgia starts Oct 13, 2026

CONFIRMED.

  • "Advance voting is required to begin October 13." (the association's letter)
  • "Early voting starts Oct. 13." (GPB)
  • https://www.documentcloud.org/documents/28738039 · https://www.gpb.org/news/2026/10/02/state-election-board-seeks-doj-dhs-intervention-in-election-equipment-update

53. The Justice Department said only that it was "aware of the letter"

CONFIRMED. (secondary only: a department spokesperson to Votebeat).

  • https://www.votebeat.org/national/2026/10/04/georgia-election-board-mills-federal-letters-dhs-doj-board-approval/

54. CISA's June 2022 advisory listed nine vulnerabilities in the touchscreen model Georgia uses, and found no evidence any was exploited in an election

CONFIRMED. By both hand passes. Exploiting them needs physical access or access to the election management system. The page is now marked as archived content.

  • "as used in Dominion Democracy Suite Voting System Version 5.5-A"
  • "While these vulnerabilities present risks that should be mitigated as soon as possible, CISA has no evidence that these vulnerabilities have been exploited in any elections."
  • "Exploitation of these vulnerabilities would require physical access to individual ImageCast X devices, access to the Election Management System (EMS), or the ability to modify files before they are uploaded to ImageCast X devices."
  • https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/ics-advisories/icsa-22-154-01

55. The federal suit over Georgia's touchscreen machines was dismissed for lack of standing on Mar 31, 2025, after a 17-day trial, with the judge calling the security concerns "substantial"

CONFIRMED. By both hand passes. The court made no finding that any vote was altered: ruling on standing, it never reached that question. Hand pass B also read the opinion's summary of SB 189 as ending QR-code tabulation from July 1, 2026; that date was later moved (entry 50).

  • "the Court concludes that Plaintiffs lack standing to pursue their claims"
  • "The Court thus lacks jurisdiction to consider the merits of Plaintiffs’ claims and must dismiss this case."
  • "their work has identified substantial concerns about the administration, maintenance, and security of Georgia’s electronic in-person voting system"
  • "BMDs can be attacked by sticking a ballpoint pen in the back of the machine to reboot the BMD in safe mode"
  • "serious security breach that occurred in Coffee County, Georgia in the aftermath of the 2020 election"
  • "CISA lists nine BMD vulnerabilities"
  • https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.240678/gov.uscourts.gand.240678.1868.0.pdf · https://www.democracydocket.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/09/1868-2025-03-31-Opinion-and-Order.pdf

56. "The case has since been dismissed" and is over

CORRECTED. The plaintiffs appealed on Apr 14, 2025 (Eleventh Circuit No. 25-11320). The latest docket entry is a May 6, 2026 appeals-court order on counsel withdrawal, and no decision appears.

  • "NOTICE OF APPEAL as to 1868 Order and 1870 Judgment"
  • https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/6139924/curling-v-raffensperger/ · https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.gand.240678/gov.uscourts.gand.240678.1902.0.pdf

57. SB 189 (2024) makes the printed text, not the QR code, the official vote for tabulation

CONFIRMED. (the rule; its start date is in entry 50). The federal judge noted it would need funding and further government action.

  • "The official tabulation count of any ballot scanner shall be based upon" … "and does not use a QR code, bar code, or similar coding"
  • https://www.legis.ga.gov/api/legislation/document/20232024/229578

58. SB 3EX (2026) also creates a committee to specify a new system based on hand-marked paper ballots, reporting by Jan 31, 2027

CONFIRMED.

  • "of election equipment based upon hand marked paper ballots produced utilizing ballot on"
  • "By January 31, 2027, the committee shall prepare a report"
  • https://www.legis.ga.gov/api/legislation/document/2026EX/249745

59. Georgia's 2024 ballot-image audit found zero differences between the QR codes and the printed text

CONFIRMED. 5,297,262 images; 5,025,863 machine-printed ballots.

  • "There were zero differences found between the tabulation based on the human-readable text and the tabulation based on the QR codes"
  • https://blog.citp.princeton.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/952/2026/04/georgia_ballot_image_audit_report-_november_general_0.pdf

60. The board's letters lean on a Sept 2, 2026 Homeland Security letter asking the Justice Department whether QR-code tabulation complies with federal law

CONFIRMED. That the letter exists and what it asks. It is a request for a legal determination; no answer was found.

  • "First, we asked DOJ to determine if voting systems that count votes from barcodes and QR codes (Quick Response Codes) are compliant with Title III of the Help America Vote Act (HAVA)" (OCR of a scan)
  • https://seb.ga.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Homeland-Security-Letter-9-2-2026.pdf

Case file: Wisconsin — the "ballots under the table" memory, and Wisconsin's real items

Tray: EXPLAINED: the clip people remember is Georgia's; the 3–4 a.m. jump was Milwaukee's absentee batch; the 2024 trucks clip; the 2022 Madison clip relabelled as Philadelphia. PROVEN as errors, found and fixed: 386 uncounted ballots found by the 2020 recount, and 31,000 ballots re-run in 2024.

61. There was no separate Wisconsin "ballots under a table" clip; that story is the Georgia footage

UNRESOLVED. Three targeted searches found no Wisconsin clip of ballots pulled from under a table, but an absence of results is not proof, so this stays off air as a flat statement. On air: the clip most people remember was filmed in Georgia. One real Wisconsin item (entry 63, ballots found underneath counted envelopes) is a plausible seed for the memory; that link is an inference, not a sourced fact.

62. Wisconsin's 2020 viral claim was "found 112k votes at 3 a.m." / "a 4 a.m. ballot dump"

CONFIRMED. It was Milwaukee's central count reporting its absentee ballots in one batch overnight. The state commission's own FAQ page on it returned an error and was not read.

  • "The City of Milwaukee was still counting absentee ballots around 3 a.m., but that doesn’t mean that those ballots were" … "or appeared at that time." (PolitiFact)
  • "It seems that these different states they found magical ballots at 4am." (the podcast video that pushed it, Nov 4, 2020)
  • https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2020/nov/04/facebook-posts/social-media-post-falsely-claims-wisconsin-found-1/ · https://leadstories.com/hoax-alert/2020/11/fact-check-episode-michigan-did-not-add-138000-votes-where-none-were-for-trump-and-there-was-not-a-4-am-ballot-dump-in-wisconsin.html

63. In the Nov 2020 Milwaukee recount, 386 uncounted absentee ballots were found underneath counted envelopes

CONFIRMED. Real, human error, found by the recount itself, and counted by a unanimous bipartisan canvass board.

  • "Milwaukee election workers discover nearly 400 unopened, uncounted absentee ballots, found underneath opened, counted absentee ballot envelopes. The mistake was discovered on the fifth day of the recount."
  • "not counting the 386 ballots on Nov. 3 was due to human error by new election inspectors."
  • "The county board of canvassers agreed, voting unanimously to allow the ballots as part of the recount."
  • https://www.fox6now.com/news/nearly-400-uncounted-ballots-found-in-wisconsin-recount

64. Nov 2024: a clip of trucks arriving overnight at Milwaukee's central count showed illegal votes

CONFIRMED. That it was fact-checked false: the city said the trucks carried legal absentee ballots, with media present. Something real happened the same night: 31,000 absentee ballots were recounted because tabulator doors had been left unlocked, with other safeguards ruling out tampering.

  • "The footage shows nothing nefarious, however. There is no proof that any ballots delivered overnight were illegal and the trucks arrived with media present."
  • "It is both expected and routine that absentee ballots—over 100,000 in this case—are counted and reported in the late hours of Election Night" (the city's election commission)
  • "election workers count 31,000 absentee ballots all over again because some staff didn’t lock tabulator doors in the early morning" … "other security measures ensured there was no chance of tampering."
  • https://dailycaller.com/2024/11/14/fact-check-no-this-video-does-not-show-illegal-votes-being-trucked-in-overnight-in-milwaukee (a syndicated copy of a Check Your Fact article) · https://votebeat.org/wisconsin/2024/11/06/election-day-madison-bomb-threat-milwaukee-absentee-ballot-recount

65. Nov 2022: a Madison poll worker initialling ballots was relabelled as "cheating in Philadelphia"

CONFIRMED. The clip was real; the place label was moved. A reverse-image search of the room identified the building. Initialling is a procedure state law requires.

  • "AP’S ASSESSMENT: False. The video shows a poll worker in Madison, Wisconsin, on Tuesday. He was initialing ballots to be handed out to voters, a standard procedure mandated by state law"
  • "The broadcast then switched to the clip of the poll worker and changed the location in the label to Madison. Social media versions of the video cropped out the location."
  • "shall write their initials on the back of each ballot and deliver to each elector as he or she enters the voting booth."
  • https://witf.org/2022/11/08/video-shows-wisconsin-poll-worker-not-cheating-in-philadelphia

66. Wisconsin's Legislative Audit Bureau (2021): 3 of 14,710 sampled absentee certificates lacked a voter signature

CONFIRMED. The same table: 8 lacked a witness signature, 15 a witness address, and 1,022 (6.9%) had partial witness addresses. 3 in 14,710 is about 1 in 4,900.

  • "We reviewed 14,710 certificates for absentee ballots cast in 29 municipalities"
  • "1,022 certificates (6.9 percent) had partial witness addresses;"
  • "3 certificates (less than 0.1 percent) did not have a voter signature."
  • https://legis.wisconsin.gov/lab/media/vxinij4f/21-19Summary.pdf

67. Eleven people who died before Nov 3, 2020 likely voted; their absentee ballots had already been mailed

CONFIRMED.

  • "11 individuals who died before November 3, 2020, likely voted in the General Election. Clerks received potential data matches for all 11 individuals 10 days before the General Election. By that point in time, clerks had mailed absentee ballots to all 11 individuals."
  • https://legis.wisconsin.gov/lab/media/rz1nj2dh/21-19full.pdf

68. The state commission's care-facility guidance did not comply with statutes

CONFIRMED. Guidance that broke statute is non-compliance, not fraud.

  • "WEC issued written guidance directing clerks not to send or attempt to send special voting deputies to facilities and homes but to instead mail absentee ballots" … "This guidance did not comply with statutes."
  • https://legis.wisconsin.gov/lab/media/vxinij4f/21-19Summary.pdf

69. "1 of 45 sworn complaints sustained"

CORRECTED. As of early June 2021, of the 34 complaints about officials' conduct or election-law violations: 25 were dismissed, 6 were not yet resolved, 2 were withdrawn, and 1 was resolved by directing an official to follow election law. "1 of 45 sustained" overstates the finality.

  • "25 complaints were dismissed by WEC or its administrator" … "6 complaints were not yet resolved;" … "2 complaints were withdrawn by the individuals who had submitted them; and" … "1 complaint was resolved by the administrator in consultation with its chairperson. The decision directed an election official to follow election laws."
  • https://legis.wisconsin.gov/lab/media/rz1nj2dh/21-19full.pdf (pp. 95–96)

70. The commission's rebuttal on care-facility ballots: 579 issued and 389 returned in 2020, against 810 and 633 in 2016

CONFIRMED.

  • "November 2020: 579 ballots issued to SVD voters; 389 ballots returned" and "November 2016: 810 ballots issued to SVD voters; 633 ballots returned"
  • https://urbanmilwaukee.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Special-Counsel-Report-Inaccuracies.pdf

71. The legislature's special counsel conceded that decertification "would not" change the result

UNRESOLVED. The special counsel's report itself was not opened, and the commission's rebuttal does not contain the phrase. Kept off air.

  • Settled in the second top-up round: the report itself said decertification would not change who is president (AP, quoting the report; entry 419).

Case file: Detroit (TCF Center, 2020) and Philadelphia — "observers locked out, windows covered, a 4 a.m. ballot dump"

Tray: EXPLAINED as a ballot dump or fraud. PROVEN as conduct problems on both sides (papered windows, challengers held at the door: "not always lawful or wise").

72. The overnight delivery was about 16,000 Election Day absentee ballots

CONFIRMED. As the Detroit adviser's estimate, in the Michigan Senate Oversight Committee's report (June 2021). Claims at the time ran from 30,000–45,000 to nearly 100,000.

  • "estimated 16,000 ballots were delivered to the TFC Center around that time. Some other persons and media speculated it was nearly 100,000, but most reported about 30,000-45,000."
  • "These ballots were submitted throughout Election Day at different locations, such as drop boxes, in the mail, and at the clerk’s main and satellite offices."
  • https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (p. 26)

73. The van arrived about 3:30 a.m. and again about 4:30 a.m.; the "wagon" photo was a TV photographer's gear

CONFIRMED.

  • "In the video, the van arrived around 3:30 a.m. and unloaded the absentee ballots."
  • "The van arrived back once again around 4:30 a.m. to unload the final ballots."
  • "it was a photo of a WXYZ-TV photographer hauling his equipment."
  • https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (pp. 26–27)
  • Detroit's elections director later explained that the late ballots were drop-box and satellite ballots collected after 8 p.m.: "And that is exactly what you experienced at 3 o’clock, 4 o’clock in the morning." https://www.kpbs.org/news/politics/2024/10/31/after-a-chaotic-vote-count-in-2020-heres-what-detroit-will-do-differently-this-year

74. The Republican-led Senate committee found no fraud, but called window-covering and removals by both sides "not always lawful or wise"

CONFIRMED.

  • "This Committee found no evidence of widespread or systematic fraud in Michigan’s prosecution of the 2020 election."
  • "such as covering windows, calling police, denying lawful challenges, and removing challengers. Those actions by both sides were not always lawful or wise"
  • "the Committee found no evidence fraudulent activities were undertaken or that such actions led to irreparable harm to ballots or vote counting."
  • https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (pp. 2, 13–14)

75. The committee recommended chain-of-custody manifests for ballots moved to the count

CONFIRMED.

  • "the process of transferring ballots from the clerk’s office to other locations must be done with greater security and manifests so that there can be an accounting for each ballot sent and received between the two locations, establishing a chain of custody."
  • https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (p. 27)

76. More than 570 challengers were in the room (227 Republican) against a cap of 134 per party, and late arrivals of both parties were held at the door

CONFIRMED. (secondary: PolitiFact relaying the Detroit Free Press and the city's lawyer).

  • "At one point, there were more than 570 challengers in the counting room, the Detroit Free Press reported, including 227 Republican challengers."
  • "election officials barred both Republican and Democratic challengers from reentering."
  • "Election workers counting ballots asked security to place paper over the windows because they felt intimidated by Republican challengers banging on the doors and windows."
  • https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2020/nov/06/facebook-posts/over-100-republican-challengers-monitored-absentee/

77. The state audit reconciled Detroit's absentee count to within 21 of about 174,000

CONFIRMED. With the wording fixed: the net was 21 more names than ballots (more voters recorded than ballots), not 21 extra ballots. About 1 in 8,300.

  • "Approximately 174,000 Absent voter ballots were cast in Detroit. Following the county canvass, 95 of 134 AV counting boards were out of balance."
  • "The net number of ballots for the entire counting board was 21 more names than ballots, out of approximately 174,000 AV votes cast."
  • https://www.michigan.gov/documents/sos/BOE_2020_Post_Election_Audit_Report_04_21_21_723005_7.pdf (p. 20)
  • The March 2021 press release gave 17; the April 2021 report's 21 is the corrected figure (entry 305). How the counting boards fell out of balance, and how many, is in entries 295–309.

78. The audit found challenges were documented, not ignored

CONFIRMED.

  • "This suggests that claims, made by some, that challenges were being ignored were not accurate."
  • https://www.michigan.gov/documents/sos/BOE_2020_Post_Election_Audit_Report_04_21_21_723005_7.pdf (p. 19, fn. 16)

79. A Wayne County court (Nov 13, 2020) found the challengers' affidavits misread the process; no challenger affiant had attended the Oct 29 walk-through

CONFIRMED. (read via a public transcription of the order; the court's PDF is a scan). It was not a final order.

  • "None of Plaintiff challengers affiants attended the session."
  • "This is not a final order and does not close the case."
  • "There is no evidentiary basis to attribute any evil activity by virtue of the city using a rental truck with out-of-state license plates."
  • https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Costantino_v._City_of_Detroit

80. The related federal suit ended in sanctions on its lawyers, reduced on appeal

CONFIRMED. (Sixth Circuit, June 23, 2023). The sanctions are about the lawyers' conduct.

  • "we reduce the City’s award to $132,810.62; and we reduce the state defendants’ fee award to $19,639.75."
  • http://www.opn.ca6.uscourts.gov/opinions.pdf/23a0134p-06.pdf

81. Philadelphia 2020: Pennsylvania's Supreme Court held (Nov 17, 2020) that the election code sets no minimum distance for observers

CONFIRMED. Observers stood about 15–18 feet away, and the court held they could observe as the code prescribes.

  • "these provisions do not set a minimum distance between authorized representatives and canvassing activities"
  • "allowed candidate representatives to observe the Board conducting its activities as prescribed under the Election Code"
  • "in each section by a distance of approximately 15-18 feet."
  • https://www.pacourts.us/Storage/media/pdfs/20210604/015809-file-10581.pdf

Case file: the New York-to-Pennsylvania "truck of completed ballots" (Oct 2020)

Tray: EXPLAINED.

CONFIRMED. (USPS Office of Inspector General report; the text layer is an OCR of a redacted scan).

  • "The above joint investigation could not corroborate" … "allegation of missing ballots. The investigation is complete and submitted for closure."

CONFIRMED.

  • "most, if not all the mail contents were packages, not letters."
  • "The seal on the trailer matched the seal put on in Bethpage, NY."
  • "This review did not reveal the presence of first class mail or ballots."

  • "Multiple Postal Service employees recall seeing less than approximately one hundred (100) ballots the entire election season"

86. The only large New York-to-Pennsylvania ballot flow found was printed general-election ballots from a Rochester printer

CONFIRMED. The OIG does not say this seeded the rumour; "may explain" is the most the episode says. The claim was launched at a Dec 1, 2020 press conference.

  • "six-hundred and fifty thousand (650,000) ballots for PA. However, these ballots were either driven by Phoenix Graphics directly to respective election office in PA, or entered into the" (mail stream in Rochester, New York)

Case file: Bucks County, Pennsylvania — the "ripped-up mail ballots" video (Oct 2024)

Tray: EXPLAINED (fabricated).

97. US intelligence, the FBI and CISA said (Oct 25, 2024) that Russian actors made and amplified it, and local officials had already debunked it

CONFIRMED. The statement names Pennsylvania, not Bucks County. It states the basis only in general terms, so it is "the agencies said", not proof a viewer can check.

  • "The IC assesses that Russian actors manufactured and amplified a recent video that falsely depicted an individual ripping up ballots in Pennsylvania, judging from information available to the IC and prior activities of other Russian influence actors, including videos and other disinformation activities. Local election officials have already debunked the video’s content."
  • https://archive.dni.gov/index.php/newsroom/press-releases/press-releases-2024/4011-odni-pr-24-24 (read via a reader copy; the page refused a direct fetch)

98. The county board's bipartisan statement the same day: the video is fake

CONFIRMED.

  • "This video is fake."
  • "The envelope and materials depicted in this video are clearly not authentic materials belonging to or distributed by the Bucks County Board of Elections."
  • https://www.buckscounty.gov/m/newsflash/home/detail/1151

99. "Later" the district attorney's office and Yardley Borough police said their investigation found it fabricated

CORRECTED. The same day, not later. The DA and police statement is dated Oct 24, 2024, the day the clip appeared.

  • "Our investigation has concluded that this video was fabricated in an attempt to undermine confidence in the upcoming election."
  • https://levittownnow.com/2024/10/24/video-showing-destruction-of-bucks-county-ballot-is-fake/ · https://politifact.com/factchecks/2024/oct/25/tweets/video-doesnt-show-an-election-worker-ripping-ballo/

100. The physical tells: glossy instead of matte, the wrong green, no return addresses, no punch hole; and Pennsylvania bars opening mail ballots before 7 a.m. on Election Day

CONFIRMED. With a source fix: the punch-hole detail is from the county spokesperson to PolitiFact, not from the AP/WHYY piece.

  • "the dark green color on the left side of the outer envelopes — it is actually more of a kelly green — and the glossiness of the inner and outer envelopes, which in reality have a matte finish"
  • "none of the envelopes in the video had voters’ return addresses written on them."
  • "election officials must wait until 7 a.m. ET on Election Day before they can begin to process ballots cast by mail"
  • "each return envelope has a punch hole so election workers can see whether the yellow secrecy envelope is inside."
  • https://whyy.org/articles/russian-actors-fake-video-mail-in-ballots-bucks-county-elections-2024/ · https://politifact.com/factchecks/2024/oct/25/tweets/video-doesnt-show-an-election-worker-ripping-ballo/

101. It was debunked within three hours

CONFIRMED. As AP's reporting, attributed to local officials and law enforcement.

  • "The video had taken off on social media Thursday but was debunked within three hours by local election officials and law enforcement after members of the public reported it."
  • https://whyy.org/articles/russian-actors-fake-video-mail-in-ballots-bucks-county-elections-2024/

102. It was staged, not made with AI

CONFIRMED. With a source fix: the expert's quote is in PolitiFact, not AP.

  • "There is nothing in this video that has the characteristics or tell-tale signs of AI-generation,"
  • "I suspect that this is simply staged."
  • https://politifact.com/factchecks/2024/oct/25/tweets/video-doesnt-show-an-election-worker-ripping-ballo/

103. It reached hundreds of thousands of views on X

CONFIRMED. (NPR).

  • "where it racked up hundreds of thousands of views."
  • https://www.npr.org/2024/10/25/nx-s1-5165593/russia-ballot-video-bucks-county

Case file: "machines flipped votes" — Antrim County 2020, the machine maker's lawsuit, and the 2024 audits

Tray: EXPLAINED for flipping. PROVEN for Antrim's human card-loading error, and for the documented vulnerabilities (no exploitation shown).

112. Antrim County's hand count came "within 12 votes of 15,718"

CORRECTED. (the denominator). The net change of 12 votes is right, but 15,718 appears in no source read. The Senate committee's table puts total presidential votes at 15,969 certified and 15,982 in the hand recount: say a 12-vote shift on about 16,000 votes.

  • "largely mirroring the machine-tabulation results from Nov. 3." (the Secretary of State, Dec 18, 2020)
  • "7. Dec. 17 22,082 16,044 5,959 9,759 244 20 15,982 Hand Recount" (the committee's table)
  • https://www.michigan.gov/sos/resources/news/2020/12/18/final-numbers-from-antrim-county-audit-continue-to-affirm-accuracy-of-election-results · https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (p. 15)

113. The scanners' own paper tapes were right on election night

CONFIRMED. In the committee's table, row 1, the tabulator tapes (the official results, not reported that night), shows 5,960 to 9,748 for the two leading candidates, close to the hand count; row 2, the clerk's computer (the unofficial results that were publicly reported), shows 7,769 to 4,509, the order reversed.

  • "Tabulator tapes- official results (Not reported on election night)"
  • "Clerk’s computer- unofficial results (publicly reported)"
  • https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (p. 15)

114. The cause was human error in loading memory cards, not a security breach, and the county restated its results four times

CONFIRMED. (the state's forensic report). The one real residual error was a single vote omitted in a village ballot question.

  • "The other 15 cards appeared to load normally, but 13 had not been updated after the ballot design changes."
  • "the Antrim County incident was not caused by a security breach. There is also no credible evidence that it was caused deliberately."
  • "the county restated its results four times to correct this and other errors"
  • "the final reported outcome is potentially incorrect due to the omission of a single vote because of a separate human error."
  • https://www.michigan.gov/documents/sos/Antrim_720623_7.pdf (pp. 3–5, 30)

115. The Senate committee called the Antrim manipulation claims "indefensible" and gave their promoters "zero credibility"

CONFIRMED. (now from the report itself).

  • "manipulated the vote by hand or electronically are indefensible."
  • "place all other statements and actions they make in a position of zero credibility."
  • https://misenategopcdn.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/99/doccuments/20210623/SMPO_2020ElectionReport.pdf (pp. 16–17)

116. Wisconsin 2024: 327,230 ballots hand-counted, a 0.0% machine error rate, decided unanimously

CONFIRMED. From the commission's press release (read via the Internet Archive; the live page refused the fetch). The full staff audit memo was not opened, so the episode says "the Commission determined", not "the audit found". About 1 ballot in 10 statewide was counted by hand.

  • "unanimously determined the error rate of the 2024 post-election voting equipment audit as 0.0%."
  • "A total of 327,230 ballots, approximately 10 % of all ballots cast statewide, were counted by hand"
  • "Conducting the voting equipment audit did uncover a small number of human errors"
  • https://elections.wi.gov/news/audit-finds-no-election-machine-errors (read via the Internet Archive)

117. Georgia's 2024 ballot-image audit: zero QR-versus-text differences, and "86 intent differences plus 1 write-in error" on the hand-marked ballots

CORRECTED. (which ballots). The single write-in adjudication error was on the machine-printed (QR) ballots, not the hand-marked ones; the 86 voter-intent differences were on the hand-marked ballots. One slip in 5,025,863 machine-printed ballots.

  • "A single discrepancy was identified in the audit with ImageCast X ballots and was traced back to a county adjudication mistake with a write-in vote (i.e. human error)."
  • Table rows: "5,025,863 1 4" (machine-printed) and "271,399 86 0" (hand-marked)
  • https://blog.citp.princeton.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/952/2026/04/georgia_ballot_image_audit_report-_november_general_0.pdf (pp. 3–4)

118. Georgia's 2024 risk-limiting audit: 442 batches, 381 with no deviation at all

CONFIRMED. (the Secretary of State's release, read via the Internet Archive).

  • "A risk limit of 5% was set"
  • "Of the 442 batches, 381 (86.1%) had no deviation from the original candidate vote totals. Out of the other 61, all fell within an expected margin of error for a hand count."
  • https://sos.ga.gov/news/georgias-2024-statewide-risk-limiting-audit-confirms-voting-system-accuracy (read via the Internet Archive)

119. Pennsylvania's audits found about six discrepancies in about 37,000 hand-counted ballots

UNRESOLVED. No Pennsylvania source was opened. Kept off air.

  • Second top-up round: no 2020 Pennsylvania figure matching this was found either; Pennsylvania's 2020 audits are in entry 538.

120. The machine maker's suit against a cable network: summary judgment on falsity (Mar 31, 2023), then a $787.5 million settlement (Apr 18, 2023)

CONFIRMED.

  • "The evidence developed in this civil proceeding demonstrates that is CRYSTAL clear that none of the Statements relating to Dominion about the 2020 election are true." (Delaware Superior Court)
  • "The parties settled for $787,500,000 — about half of Dominion's original $1.6 billion ask." (NPR)
  • https://courts.delaware.gov/Opinions/Download.aspx?id=345820 · https://www.npr.org/2023/04/18/1170339114/fox-news-dominion-defamation-trial-settle

Case file: Arizona 2020 — "Sharpiegate" and the state Senate's contractor review

Tray: EXPLAINED for Sharpies and the contractor's fraud claims. PROVEN at small scale: one accidental deceased-voter instance, two double votes referred, 37 possible double votes forwarded, and chain-of-custody paperwork the Attorney General flagged.

121. Sharpies were officially issued, and the Attorney General's office said it was "now confident" they disenfranchised no one

CONFIRMED. (reporting of the office's Nov 5, 2020 statement). The county's ballots have offset columns, so bleed-through does not affect tabulation.

  • "we are now confident that the use of Sharpie markers did not result in disenfranchisement for Arizona voters."
  • "Those ballots have offset columns so if a voter marks the ballot and the ink bleeds through to the backside it would not impact tabulations." (the county spokesperson)
  • https://digital-release.newsnationnow.com/?p=1527149 · https://cronkitenews.azpbs.org/2020/11/05/hobbs-learns-sharpie-ballot-rumor-like-markers-ink-is-hard-to-erase/

122. Maricopa County rated the contractor's 76 claims: 22 misleading, 41 inaccurate, 13 false

CONFIRMED.

  • "22 were misleading." / "41 were inaccurate." / "13 were false."
  • https://web.archive.org/web/2022/https://s3.documentcloud.org/documents/21528642/correcting-the-record-january-2022-report.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

123. Of 53,304 ballots the contractor questioned, 87 were potentially illegal, and 37 possible double votes went to the Attorney General

CONFIRMED. Plus a county admission the engine left out: 50 ballots potentially double counted. 87 in 53,304 is about 1 in 600.

  • "Our analysis found 37 instances where a voter may have unlawfully cast multiple ballots. We have forwarded these instances to the Arizona Attorney General’s Office"
  • "We also found 50 instances in which a ballot was potentially double counted."
  • https://web.archive.org/web/2022/https://s3.documentcloud.org/documents/21528642/correcting-the-record-january-2022-report.pdf (pp. 4–5)

124. The contractor's own hand count "largely matched", the Senate's machine count "confirms" the county's total, and the parties' hand-count audit had found a 100% match

CONFIRMED.

  • "Cyber Ninjas reported that its hand count and the Senate’s machine count largely matched Maricopa County’s official canvass."
  • "These results show the machine count confirms Maricopa County’s reported ballots total for the 2020 election."
  • "found a 100% match to the vote tabulation equipment."
  • https://web.archive.org/web/2022/https://s3.documentcloud.org/documents/21528642/correcting-the-record-january-2022-report.pdf (pp. 5, 13)

  • "Of the 430 investigations, 22 cases were submitted for prosecution."
