Case file: the US Postal Service in 2020 — "sorting machines and mailboxes pulled to stop mail ballots" (the claim from the other side)

Tray: EXPLAINED as a plot against mail ballots. PROVEN as real machine and box removals and slower service, not shown to be aimed at ballots.

129. 712 sorting machines were removed from Oct 2019 to Aug 2020, against about 375 a year, 437 of them in June–August

CONFIRMED. (USPS Office of Inspector General).

  • "removal of letter and flat processing machines across the country increased from an average of 375 removals a year from FY 2015 to FY 2019. Between October 2019 and August of 2020, 712 letter and flat processing machines were reduced – 437 (61 percent) of these machines were removed from June to August 2020"
  • https://www.oversight.gov/sites/default/files/oig-reports/20-292-R21.pdf (p. 2)

130. The changes were made without a study of the impact on service, to hit financial targets

CONFIRMED. The OIG does not say the removals were aimed at ballots (its text was checked for any such finding).

  • "it did not complete a study or analysis of the impact the changes would make on mail service prior to implementation."
  • "were designed to achieve financial targets."
  • https://www.oversight.gov/sites/default/files/oig-reports/20-292-R21.pdf (pp. 2, 15)

131. 776 of over 140,000 blue collection boxes were removed in summer 2020; the Western area jumped from 8 a month to 187 in August; removals were suspended Aug 18

CONFIRMED. Removals overall were below the yearly average; the spike was regional. About 1 box in 180.

  • "The Postal Service has over 140,000 blue collection boxes"
  • "an additional 776 boxes from June 15, 2020 through August 31, 2020."
  • "collection box removals until after the 2020 election to avoid the appearance of any impact on Election Mail." (USPS's stated reason, not a finding)
  • https://www.oversight.gov/sites/default/files/oig-reports/20-292-R21.pdf (pp. 4, 14–15)

132. On-time mail fell about 10 points in July 2020

CONFIRMED.

  • "First-Class Single Piece declined from 90.1 to 79.7 (10.4 percentage points)."
  • https://www.oversight.gov/sites/default/files/oig-reports/21-014-R21.pdf (p. 2)

133. Election mail then ran better than other mail: 93.8% on time against 88.1%, 98.1% in election week, and the 760 delayed pieces found were all delivered by Election Day

CONFIRMED. Only 52.9% of identifiable ballots could be measured.

  • "Election Mail 133,772,172 93.8%" and "All Other First-Class Mail 6,395,986,390 88.1%"
  • "Postal Service was only able to measure service performance for 71.5 million (or 52.9 percent) of the 135 million identifiable ballots"
  • "During the week of the general election, 98.1 percent of identifiable ballots were processed in time to meet its service standard."
  • "we only identified 760 delayed Election Mail mailpieces, all of which were delivered to election offices on or before election day."
  • https://www.uspsoig.gov/sites/default/files/reports/2023-01/20-318-R21.pdf (pp. 2–6, 11)

134. USPS's own figure: 99.89% of ballots delivered within seven days

CONFIRMED. As USPS grading itself; paired on air with the OIG's figures.

  • "99.89 percent of identified ballots mailed after September 4 were delivered within seven"
  • https://about.usps.com/newsroom/national-releases/2021/0119-usps-releases-updated-2020-post-election-analysis-report.pdf

Case file: 2024 from the other side — "20 million missing votes", Starlink, "statistical anomalies"

Tray: EXPLAINED (20 million missing, satellite internet, the anomalies model).

135. Certified totals: the losing 2024 ticket got 75,017,613 votes of 155,238,302; the winning 2020 ticket had 81,268,924 of 158,383,403, a gap of about 6.25 million

CONFIRMED. From the Federal Election Commission's certified results. 81,268,924 − 75,017,613 = 6,251,311; total turnout fell by 3,145,101; the other ticket rose by 3,086,426 (our arithmetic). The viral 20 million was more than three times the real gap.

  • https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/2024presgeresults.pdf · https://www.fec.gov/resources/cms-content/documents/2020presgeresults.pdf
  • Correction, 2026-10-13: the FEC's later Federal Elections 2020 report (October 2022) gives 81,283,501 and 74,223,975, so the gap to 2024 is 6,265,888 (entry 435).

136. The "20 million" came from partial counts, and the same number was used both ways

CONFIRMED. One camp used it as proof 2024 was stolen, the other as proof 2020 had 20 million fake votes.

  • "The posts making the claim use preliminary vote counts that don't show up-to-date or final results."
  • "Posts are using the comparison between the tallies as evidence of fraud during the 2020 election"
  • "These claims are inaccurate. There have been no credible allegations of election fraud or evidence of votes that disappeared during the 2024 election."
  • https://www.aap.com.au/factcheck/no-harris-didnt-get-20-million-fewer-votes-than-biden/ · https://www.aljazeera.com/news/2024/11/9/fact-check-did-20-million-democratic-votes-disappear

137. State boards said satellite internet was not used to tabulate or upload counts, and CISA saw no material malicious activity

CONFIRMED.

  • "Satellite-based internet devices were not used to tabulate or upload vote counts in North Carolina,"
  • "no evidence of any malicious activity that had a material impact on the security or integrity of our election infrastructure." (the CISA director, Nov 6, 2024)
  • "Counties do not use Starlink to transmit unofficial or official election results. No voting system in Pennsylvania is ever connected to the internet."
  • https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2024/nov/12/threads-posts/no-elon-musks-starlink-wasnt-used-to-rig-the-2024/ · https://leadstories.com/hoax-alert/2024/11/elon-musks-starlink-did-not-play-role-in-counting-votes-in-2024-election.html

138. Satellite internet connected pollbooks only in Tulare County, California

CORRECTED. (incomplete). Arizona's Secretary of State also deployed it in three counties to sync electronic pollbooks. The real thing underneath is pollbook connectivity, not tabulation, and some tabulators do briefly connect to transmit results.

  • "As part of a pilot program to enhance connectivity in underserved areas, our office has deployed Starlink systems in Coconino, Apache, and Navajo Counties to support ePollbook"
  • "Our voting machines are never connected to the Internet, not via Starlink or anything else." (Tulare County)
  • "In some states, ballot tabulators are briefly connected to the internet to transmit results when polls close."
  • https://leadstories.com/hoax-alert/2024/11/elon-musks-starlink-did-not-play-role-in-counting-votes-in-2024-election.html · https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2024/nov/12/threads-posts/no-elon-musks-starlink-wasnt-used-to-rig-the-2024/

139. The "statistical anomalies" group has no confirming audit, recount or ruling

CORRECTED. There is a ruling. A federal judge in the Western District of Pennsylvania (June 12, 2026) dismissed with prejudice every request to recount, audit or disturb the certified 2024 results, on standing rather than the merits; refiled forward-looking claims were still pending in October 2026. Votebeat (Sept 3, 2026) found that the model behind the Pennsylvania claim flagged fraud in simulated data built to contain none.

  • "All retrospective requests for relief, including any claim seeking to disturb, recount, or audit the certified results of the November 5, 2024 General Election, or to obtain a judicial determination of the accuracy of reported vote totals in that election, are dismissed with prejudice."
  • "All said they doubt the model can accurately detect fraud in U.S. elections."
  • "using simulated data built to contain no fraud. The model still found fraud at levels similar to"
  • "The ETA’s leaders acknowledge that statistical analysis alone can’t prove fraud."
  • https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/71883352/election-truth-alliance-v-schmidt/ · https://www.votebeat.org/national/2026/09/03/election-truth-alliance-walter-mebane-2024-election-fraud-model/
  • The same researcher's 2020 paper used that model to call the 2020 Milwaukee and Allegheny estimates "negligble amounts that probably neither reﬂect nor result from bad acts." (sic). A model that reports fraud in clean data cannot settle a real election in either direction (entries 317–319). http://websites.umich.edu/~wmebane/inapB.pdf

140. A 10.54% vs 1.07% down-ballot drop-off in Clark County, Nevada

UNRESOLVED. A single advocacy-adjacent secondary source, not opened. Kept off air.

Case file: dead voters (Georgia 2020)

Tray: PROVEN at four ballots, all cast by relatives. EXPLAINED for thousands. OPEN — Homeland Security's 42,776 deceased on Georgia's rolls: (a) the method is unpublished and no count of ballots cast in those names exists; (b) the match method and list, and the state's own check, would settle it; (c) Homeland Security and Georgia's Secretary of State hold them. It is about rolls, not ballots.

141. Jan 6, 2021: 10,315 dead voters alleged; the state had found 2 potential cases

  • "original allegation that there were 1200 dead" … "We found five that" … "were all voted by close family members" (a board member)

143. The final count of dead-voter ballots is unsettled: 2, 4 or 5

CORRECTED. It is four, by the Secretary of State's sworn testimony of June 21, 2022; the "five" was a board member's interim tally while cases were open. Four in 5,000,585 hand-counted ballots is about one in 1.25 million. No list of referrals or prosecution outcomes was found.

  • "We had found 2 dead people when I wrote my letter to Congress that is dated January 6th, and subsequent to that we found 2 more. That is--1, 2, 3--4 people, not 4,000, but just a total of 4, not 10,000, not 5,000."

145. The "5,000" figure came from the then-President's own recorded call of Jan 2, 2021

CONFIRMED. (the call audio, played at the congressional hearing). On air: the claim, as made on a recorded call to the Secretary of State.

146. A federal letter now says citizenship checks found "more than 42,000 deceased registrants" on Georgia's 2026 rolls

UNRESOLVED. It is an assertion in a Sept 2, 2026 letter from the Homeland Security Secretary to the Attorney General, with no published method or data, not a finding. A registrant still on a roll is not a ballot cast. It is likely to recirculate before Nov 3.

  • "identified more than 42,000 deceased registrants on Georgia's state voter rolls."

Case file: stale voter rolls — real, lawful by design, and a roll entry is not a ballot

Tray: EXPLAINED as evidence of fraud: stale entries are real and lawful by design, and a roll entry is not a ballot.

147. In 2024 the rolls held about 234.5 million registrations against 158.2 million ballots counted

CONFIRMED. Both are the Election Assistance Commission's own figures; the same table gives 244,271,230 voting-age citizens.

  • "there were 234,504,358 individuals who were registered to vote in the United States as of November 5, 2024"
  • "States also reported that 158,211,780 voters cast ballots that were counted for the 2024 general election."
  • https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/2024_EAVS_Report_508c.pdf

148. 23.2 million (9.9%) registrations were inactive

CORRECTED. (the percentage). The count and the meaning are right (inactive voters must verify their address before they can vote), but 9.9% is a division the engine did itself. The commission's published share is 10.6%, computed without the six jurisdictions that do not separate active from inactive voters. Say about 23 million, roughly one in ten.

  • "23,184,185 were considered inactive voters (meaning they required address verification under the provisions of the NVRA before they would be permitted to vote)"
  • "2024 244,271,230 234,504,358 211,144,275 86.6% 90.0% 23,184,185 10.6%"
  • "six states (Guam, Idaho, Minnesota, New Hampshire, North Dakota, and Puerto Rico) did not distinguish between active and inactive voters"
  • https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/2024_EAVS_Report_508c.pdf

149. 21.3 million records were removed in 2022–24, about one in five because the voter had died

CONFIRMED. Including the Feb 12, 2026 errata, which revised deaths to 4,576,275 (about 4.6 million). The most common reason for removal was the federal confirmation process itself.

  • "Between the close of registration for the 2022 general election and the close of registration for the 2024 general election, states reported removing 21,298,175 records from their voter lists."
  • "Indiana provided updated total number of voters removed because they were deceased" (the errata)
  • "The most common reason was failing to return a confirmation notice"
  • https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/2024_EAVS_Report_508c.pdf · errata on https://www.eac.gov/research-and-data/datasets-codebooks-and-surveys

150. 38.8 million confirmation notices were sent, 68.9% unreturned

CONFIRMED. As the errata figures. The June 2025 report had said 39,670,903 and 69.7%; the Feb 2026 errata (a Maryland correction) changed them.

  • "Nationally, 38,803,086 confirmation notices were sent between the 2022 general election and the month before the 2024 general election" … "unreturned confirmation notices accounted for 68.9% of the total confirmation notices sent."
  • https://www.eac.gov/research-and-data/datasets-codebooks-and-surveys (errata) · https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/2024_EAVS_Report_508c.pdf

151. Georgia removed 466,063 records (5.7%), 146,022 for death, against 5,297,500 ballots counted

CONFIRMED. (Georgia was untouched by either errata).

  • "Georgia 466,063 5.7% 34,769 7.5% 146,022 31.3% 174,396 37.4%"
  • "Georgia 159 7,174,961 7,917,054 5,297,500 73.8% 66.9%"
  • https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/2024_EAVS_Report_508c.pdf

152. The count already rejects mail ballots from voters who died

CONFIRMED. (the rate). Of 584,463 rejected mail ballots in 2024, 1.5% were rejected because the voter had died and 11.1% because the voter had already voted. Multiplying those out is not the commission's figure, so the size is APPROXIMATE: say "thousands". A ballot rejected because the voter died is usually a real voter who mailed a ballot and then died: evidence the check runs, not of fraud.

  • "47,629,437 were reported as being counted and 584,463 were reported as being rejected"
  • "Voter was deceased 1.5%" and "Voter already cast another ballot that was accepted 11.1%"
  • https://www.eac.gov/sites/default/files/2025-06/2024_EAVS_Report_508c.pdf

153. Federal law: a mover can't be dropped without written confirmation, or a missed notice plus two federal general elections; removal for not voting is barred; systematic removals end 90 days before a federal election

CONFIRMED. (52 U.S.C. § 20507).

  • "shall not result in the removal of the name of any person from the official list of voters registered to vote in an election for Federal office by reason of the person’s failure to vote"
  • "A State shall complete, not later than 90 days prior to the date of a primary or general election for Federal office , any program the purpose of which is to systematically remove the names of ineligible voters"
  • "conduct a general program that makes a reasonable effort to remove the names of ineligible voters from the official lists of eligible voters by reason of— (A) the death of the registrant; or (B) a change in the residence of the registrant"
  • https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/52/20507

154. ERIC matches roll, licence, death and change-of-address data, reports to its member states, and never touches the rolls; 27 states and DC are members

CONFIRMED. One answer on its FAQ page still says 24 states plus DC; the home page and members list are current.

  • "ERIC is never connected to any state’s voter registration system. Members retain complete control over their voter rolls."
  • "Deceased Report: Identifies voters who have died using voter registration data and Social Security death data known as the Limited Access Death Master File and MVD data."
  • "Possible cases of illegal voting are reviewed and, if found credible, referred to law enforcement."
  • https://ericstates.org/ · https://ericstates.org/?p=987

155. Pew (2012): about 24 million inaccurate registrations, 1.8 million deceased people listed, 2.75 million people registered in more than one state

CONFIRMED. As 2012 data, labelled as old. These are roll records, not ballots.

  • "Approximately 24 million—one of every eight—voter registrations in the United States are no longer valid or are significantly inaccurate."
  • "More than 1.8 million deceased individuals are listed as voters."
  • "Approximately 2.75 million people have registrations in more than one state."
  • https://www.pew.org/en/research-and-analysis/reports/2012/02/14/inaccurate-costly-and-inefficient-evidence-that-americas-voter-registration-system-needs-an-upgrade

Case file: noncitizens on the rolls — registered is not voted

Tray: PROVEN in small numbers (Georgia 20 registered in 2024; Utah 13 who had voted; Texas 117 referred). OPEN — SAVE's error rate: (a) 28,635+ flags with no published false-positive rate; (b) the resolution of every flag would settle it; (c) Homeland Security/USCIS and the states hold it.

156. Georgia's October 2024 citizenship audit found 20 noncitizens registered out of 8.2 million

CONFIRMED. (registered). How many had voted is UNRESOLVED in this record: AP's same-day story said none had voted in that November's election and past voting was still being checked; a Georgia Recorder story the next day said nine had voted in earlier elections, but the primary release was not obtained, so the nine is not aired.

  • "a check of voter rolls found that 20 of the 8.2 million people registered to vote in the state are not U.S. citizens"
  • "none of those people has cast a ballot in November’s general election but that an investigation into whether they had voted in past elections was ongoing"
  • "nine of the 20 noncitizens identified in the citizenship audit had voted illegally in previous Georgia elections"
  • https://www.wabe.org/georgia-citizenship-audit-finds-few-noncitizens-on-voter-rolls/ · https://alabamareflector.com/2024/10/24/georgia-gop-secretary-of-state-audit-found-20-noncitizens-registered-to-vote-out-of-8-2m/

157. Georgia, Sept 1, 2026: "432 potential noncitizens who have voted at some point since 1996"

CONFIRMED. As stated by the Secretary of State's office: "potential", over 30 years, with registrations going back to 1948. It is not a count of proven illegal votes.

  • "the Secretary of State’s Office identified 432 potential noncitizens who have voted at some point since 1996, including records identified by DOAA."
  • "Registration dates for these records go back to 1948, before Georgia required proof of citizenship to register to vote, and some records have not voted since the 1990s."
  • "just 0.1% of the voter registration records would have allowed an individual to vote with an unverified identity, address, or citizenship."
  • https://sos.ga.gov/news/state-audit-proves-georgia-national-leader-clean-voter-lists (read via a reader copy)

158. Utah's citizenship review (final report May 27, 2026): 27 confirmed noncitizens registered, 13 of whom had voted, out of 2,069,640 records

CONFIRMED. A further 25 were "likely noncitizens", 9 of whom had voted; 5,007 records were unconfirmed, mostly old ones lacking ID numbers. About 1 in 160,000 records had a confirmed noncitizen who voted (our arithmetic).

  • "In total, our office reviewed 2,069,640 voter records."
  • "While we found information obtained from the DLD or SAVE to be reliable in confirming whether an individual is a citizen, it was not reliable in indicating an individual was not a citizen, without considering other factors."
  • https://ltgovernor.utah.gov/wp-content/uploads/CITIZENSHIP-FULL-SUMMARY-2.pdf

159. The federal SAVE system, run against state rolls in 2025–26, verified 65 million voters and flagged 28,635 as potential noncitizens

CONFIRMED. As the government's claim in its Supreme Court filing, as NPR reported it. Its error rate is UNRESOLVED: none has been published. A district judge blocked the modified system in June 2026, and the Supreme Court revived it for now on Sept 25, 2026, with three Justices dissenting. The federal fact sheet says SAVE does not decide who may vote.

  • "since May 2025, the updated version of SAVE has verified more than 65 million voters across 26 states, and has flagged 28,635 as potential noncitizens"
  • "the government had not yet released the system's error rate"
  • "SAVE provides verification information but does not determine an individual’s eligibility to register to vote, stay on a voting roll, or vote in an election." (USCIS)
  • "In at least five different states, the system has flagged people as noncitizens who were actually citizens." (Votebeat)
  • https://www.npr.org/2026/09/25/nx-s1-5976804/supreme-court-trump-save-noncitizen-voting · https://www.dhs.gov/news/2026/09/25/dhs-applauds-supreme-court-decision-permitting-citizenship-verification-voters · https://www.uscis.gov/save/current-user-agencies/guidance/voter-registration-and-voter-list-maintenance-fact-sheet · https://www.uscis.gov/newsroom/news-releases/uscis-enhances-voter-verification-systems · https://www.votebeat.org/national/2026/09/25/supreme-court-ruling-save-system-trump-dhs-noncitizens-2026-election/

160. Texas ran 18 million registrations through SAVE: 2,724 flagged, 578 later shown to be citizens, 117 cases referred for investigation

CONFIRMED. (the Texas Secretary of State, Sept 15, 2026). One flag in five fell away on review, mostly because passport data reached SAVE after the first run. 117 in 18 million is about 1 in 150,000.

  • "ran the names of the state’s 18 million registered voters through the federal Systematic Alien Verification for Entitlements (SAVE) database — a process that identified 2,724 potential noncitizens on the voter rolls"
  • "The additional review showed that 578 voters demonstrated citizenship. Of those, citizenship was proven for 506 because passport information was added to SAVE after the state’s initial run."
  • "today referred 117 cases to the Office of the Attorney General for investigation of potential noncitizen voting"
  • https://www.sos.state.tx.us/about/newsreleases/2026/091526.shtml
  • Correction, 2026-10-13: Texas first announced the 2,724 on Oct 20, 2025; the Sept 15, 2026 release is the follow-up (entry 263).

161. SAVE has flagged real citizens

CONFIRMED. (NPR, Dec 2025). NPR documented a naturalised citizen removed after missing a 30-day notice; he is a private person and is not named.

  • "Eligible voters have been erroneously flagged by SAVE, which has been unable to confirm the citizenship of certain categories of foreign-born people who become U.S. citizens."
  • "Louisiana found 79 likely noncitizens voted in elections going back to the 1980s, Tennessee referred 42 potential noncitizens who cast ballots to the FBI, and Indiana said it found"
  • https://www.npr.org/2025/12/10/nx-s1-5588384/save-voting-data-us-citizens · https://www.npr.org/2026/09/25/nx-s1-5976804/supreme-court-trump-save-noncitizen-voting

Case file: the Justice Department's 2025–26 suits for states' full voter rolls

Tray: EXPLAINED for reading the dismissals as the rolls are clean: they rule on the department's power to demand the data.

162. 31 suits (30 states plus DC): 26 lost, Oklahoma resolved, 4 pending (Delaware, Hawaii, Idaho, Utah)

CONFIRMED. (CBS, updated Oct 1, 2026, and a university tracker updated Oct 7, 2026; re-check on script day). These are rulings on the department's legal power to demand the data, not findings that rolls are clean.

  • "the Justice Department has filed lawsuits against 30 states and the District of Columbia in an effort to force them to hand over the voter data."
  • "The other cases remain pending. Those are in Delaware, Hawaii, Idaho and Utah."
  • "Of the Justice Department’s 31 lawsuits, 26 have been dismissed on the merits by district courts."
  • "To date, no court at either the district or appellate level has ruled in favor of the Justice Department."
  • https://www.cbsnews.com/news/trump-lawsuits-state-voter-rolls-justice-department/ · https://statedemocracy.law.wisc.edu/our-work/tracker-doj-lawsuits-seeking-states-sensitive-voter-data · https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/justice-department-sues-six-additional-states-failure-provide-voter-registration-rolls
  • Update, 2026-10-13: on Oct 9, 2026 a federal judge vacated the Justice Department's voter-roll program itself (entry 268); the state-by-state record and the states' reasons are in entries 250–277.

163. The Sixth Circuit (Michigan) affirmed dismissal 2–1 on June 24, 2026

CONFIRMED. Two grounds: the state's voter file is internally generated, not a record that "came into" the official's possession, and the demand lacked the required statement of its basis and purpose. Rehearing by the full court was denied on Aug 14, 2026.

  • "The district court dismissed the suit, concluding that Title III’s narrow text cannot withstand the weight of the government’s broad request. We agree and affirm."
  • "So it is an internally generated electronic database, not a record acquired from an outside source"
  • "statement of both the basis and purpose of its request"
  • "less than a majority of the judges voted in favor of rehearing en banc. Therefore, the petition is denied."
  • https://www.opn.ca6.uscourts.gov/opinions.pdf/26a0180p-06.pdf · https://www.opn.ca6.uscourts.gov/opinions.pdf/26a0233p-06.pdf

164. Georgia's refiled suit was dismissed Sept 30, 2026: the judge held the 1960 Act does not authorise the demand or preempt state privacy law, and that Georgia's public roll for a fee "would be enough"

CORRECTED. The order rests on one ground (the 1960 Act does not preempt Georgia's voter-privacy law) and expressly declines to decide whether the demand was sufficient. The phrase "would be enough" is not in the order. The order also records that Georgia had already handed over a redacted list. And the first Georgia suit was dismissed for lack of subject-matter jurisdiction, not for wrong venue (N.D. Ga. No. 1:26-cv-00485, Doc. 146).

  • "The Court finds that the first issue raised by Defendant, that the CRA does not preempt Georgia’s voter privacy law, is dispositive, particularly given that Defendant largely complied with Plaintiff’s demand for voter records. Therefore, the Court does not need to reach the other issues raised by the Parties regarding the sufficiency of Plaintiff’s demand"
  • "That case was then dismissed without prejudice on January 23, 2026, for lack of subject matter jurisdiction."
  • Order of the U.S. District Court for the Northern District of Georgia, Sept 30, 2026, No. 1:26-cv-00485 (Doc. 146)

165. Maryland: the judge "joins every court to have addressed this issue"

CONFIRMED. (U.S. District Court for the District of Maryland, No. SAG-25-3934, June 18, 2026).

  • "Accordingly, this Court joins every court to have addressed this issue in concluding that an SVRL is not a record or paper that a state must produce to the United States under the CRA."

Case file: North Carolina's 9th District, 2018 — real, proven, organised fraud

Tray: PROVEN: an organised absentee-ballot operation; the election was rerun on a 5–0 bipartisan vote.

166. The bipartisan State Board voted 5-0 for a new election; it could "not determine who would have won", and the irregularities tainted the whole race

CONFIRMED. With a sourcing fix: the "could not determine who would have won" wording is from the Board's March 11, 2019 question-and-answer document, not the order. The order says the number of affected votes could not be fixed and did not need to be.

  • "the 2018 General Election for North Carolina’s 9th Congressional District was corrupted by fraud, improprieties, and irregularities so pervasive that its results are tainted"
  • "Tampering, obstruction and disguise have obscured the precise number of votes either unlawfully counted or excluded, but substantial evidence supports our conclusion that the absentee ballot scheme and other irregularities cast doubt on the outcome"
  • "It is neither required nor possible for the State Board to determine the precise number of ballots affected in circumstances such as this."
  • "Because the State Board of Elections found that the election was tainted and could not determine who would have won the election if not for absentee voting and other irregularities, it did not certify a winner."
  • "The vote to hold a new election was unanimous – three Democrats and two Republicans."
  • https://dl.ncsbe.gov/index.html?prefix=State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/ (the Board's order of Mar 13, 2019) · https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/Final_QA_9thCD_03112019_.pdf

167. The operation submitted at least 788 absentee request forms in Bladen County and at least 231 in Robeson, and paid workers $125 per 50 ballots collected

CONFIRMED. It also paid $150 per 50 request forms.

  • "at least 788 absentee by mail request forms in Bladen County were submitted by"
  • "at least 231 absentee by mail request forms in Robeson County were submitted by"
  • "though an email suggests the number may have been at least 449."
  • "he would pay them $150.00 per 50 absentee ballot request forms collected and $125.00 per 50 absentee ballots collected"
  • https://dl.ncsbe.gov/index.html?prefix=State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/ (the order, findings 37, 41, 42)

168. The margin was 905 votes; mail-in absentee votes in the two counties' parts of the district totalled 1,364

CONFIRMED. For the 905 and the county figures; the 1,364 total is our sum of the Board's slide figures (684 + 680). Districtwide, more than 10,500 mail ballots were counted. This is the one case in the file where the pool of suspect ballots exceeded the margin.

  • "led by a margin of 905 votes, or 0.3% of the total number of votes tallied."
  • "The number of returned absentee by mail ballots far exceeded the margin between"
  • https://dl.ncsbe.gov/index.html?prefix=State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/ (the order) · https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/Executive%20Director%27s%20Preview%20of%20the%20Evidence.pdf

169. Unreturned absentee ballots: Bladen 595 of 1,323 (44.97%); Robeson 1,493 of 2,269 (65.80%)

CONFIRMED. (the executive director's preview of the evidence). Unreturned is not stolen.

  • https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/Executive%20Director%27s%20Preview%20of%20the%20Evidence.pdf (slide 5)

170. It was caught by investigators and records, not by a viral clip; the Board refused to certify on Nov 27, 2018

CONFIRMED. The investigation ran 142 completed voter interviews and subpoenaed financial and phone records.

  • "This team opened an investigation based on reports from Bladen County. After gathering evidence, investigators presented preliminary information to the former nine-member State Board of Elections and Ethics Enforcement on November 27. As a result, the former nine-member Board unanimously declined to certify the results"
  • "142 completed voter interviews (401 total attempts)"
  • "Subpoenas of documents and other records including financial records and phone records"
  • https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/Final_QA_9thCD_03112019_.pdf · https://s3.amazonaws.com/dl.ncsbe.gov/State_Board_Meeting_Docs/Congressional_District_9_Portal/Executive%20Director%27s%20Preview%20of%20the%20Evidence.pdf

171. The prosecutions ended in misdemeanour pleas without jail; the operative died before trial; the candidate was not charged

CONFIRMED. (AP via NBC, WSOC and Yahoo; secondary). Four people pleaded guilty in Sept 2022, with later pleas in Nov–Dec 2022 and community service. The operative died in April 2022 before his trial; his only conviction was federal and unrelated to ballots. The candidate, who did not run in the rerun, was not charged.

  • "Four people pleaded guilty on Monday to misdemeanors for their roles in absentee ballot fraud in rural North Carolina during the 2016 and 2018 elections" … "which resulted in no active prison or jail time."
  • "He rejected a plea agreement and looked forward to his day in court, according to a friend. But he died in April after being diagnosed with lung cancer."
  • "pleaded guilty in June 2021 to obtaining illegal Social Security benefits while concealing payments for political work he performed."
  • https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/elections/four-people-plead-guilty-north-carolina-ballot-probe-2016-2018-electio-rcna49534 · https://www.wsoctv.com/news/local/5-remaining-9th-district-election-fraud-suspects-face-judge-monday/MTZTEMIMPJE7FDAZV5ZPPL3J3I/ · https://www.yahoo.com/news/nc-election-fraud-suspect-pleads-230422677.html

Case file: Bridgeport, Connecticut, 2023 — real ballot-stuffing on camera, proven in court

Tray: PROVEN: partisans handling other people's absentee ballots on drop-box video; the primary was voided; nine people charged.

172. The primary's margin was 251 votes (4,212 to 3,961); the challenger won the in-person vote and the incumbent the absentee vote

CONFIRMED. (Superior Court decision, Nov 1, 2023, Docket FBT-CV-23-6127336-S; an OCR of a scan). In person: 2,648 to 3,100; absentee: 1,564 to 861. The absentee pool (2,425) was nearly ten times the margin.

  • "The results of the primary election as reported to the state were 4,212 votes for" [the incumbent] "and 3,961 votes for" [the challenger]
  • "1,564 absentee ballot votes" … "861 absentee ballot votes"
  • https://civilinquiry.jud.ct.gov/DocumentInquiry/DocumentInquiry.aspx?DocumentNo=26199518

173. The footage: 21 days of drop-box video, 420 approaches, several people dropping multiple ballots per trip

CONFIRMED.

  • "The Bridgeport Police produced videos of the recordings of the activity at the four Drop Boxes for a 21 -day period that absentee ballots were cast in this primary."
  • "420 discreet approaches were made to the four Drop Boxes by individuals during the relevant period of video produced."
  • "The video clips from the drop boxes shows several people dropping multiple ballots into the drop box during a single approach to the drop box."
  • https://civilinquiry.jud.ct.gov/DocumentInquiry/DocumentInquiry.aspx?DocumentNo=26199518 (p. 21)

174. Two campaign figures, the town party's vice-chair and a council candidate, took the Fifth; the court found they made multiple drops of multiple ballots

CONFIRMED. (CT Mirror's relay of the findings; the court text is an OCR).

  • "appear multiple times in the video clips that were accepted into evidence making multiple drops of multiple ballots each time into the Drop Boxes."
  • https://ctmirror.org/?p=1056745

175. The ruling (Nov 1, 2023) ordered a new primary, with the result "in serious doubt"

CONFIRMED. The legal finding is unlawful handling of real voters' absentee ballots by partisans who were not allowed to handle them, which under Connecticut law voids those ballots whether or not fraud is proven. It is not a finding of fake ballots.

  • "The videos are shocking to the court and should be shocking to all the parties."
  • "established substantial violations of 9-140b in the placing of absentee ballots into drop boxes by partisans who were not designated to handle such ballots and that the volume of ballots so mishandled is such that it calls the result of the primary" … "election in serious doubt and leaves the court unable to determine the legitimate result of the primary."
  • "The validity of the ballot, therefore, depends not on whether there has been fraud, but on" (quoting state precedent)
  • https://civilinquiry.jud.ct.gov/DocumentInquiry/DocumentInquiry.aspx?DocumentNo=26199518 (pp. 36–37)

176. Up to 1,253 drop-box ballots against about 420 people on 2,100 hours of video

APPROXIMATE. This is the plaintiff's argument as reported, not a court finding; the 1,253 is not aired as fact.

  • "arguing that as many as 1,253 were submitted through drop boxes, while 2,100 hours of surveillance videos of the city's four drop boxes show only about 420 people placing ballots in them."
  • https://ctmirror.org/?p=1056745

177. In the court-ordered rerun (Jan 23, 2024), the incumbent won again

CONFIRMED.

  • "claimed victory over challenger" … "in a court-ordered second primary today."
  • https://ctmirror.org/?p=1064553

178. Nine people were charged over the 2023 cycle after a state elections-commission referral; two rejected plea deals in Aug 2026; no verdict yet

CONFIRMED. Five were charged on Feb 21, 2025 and four more on July 30, 2025; the party vice-chair faces 42 counts of unlawful ballot possession among others. They are charged, not convicted. An earlier case, over the 2019 primary, produced a guilty plea in July 2025 from a canvasser for the other faction, so absentee abuse in the city was not one camp's. A new charge over the 2026 primary was announced on Oct 6, 2026 (a Connecticut Division of Criminal Justice press release whose web address carries the defendant's name, so it is not linked here).

  • "five people have been criminally charged in connection with the misuse of absentee ballots during the 2023 Bridgeport Democratic mayoral election cycle."
  • "The SEEC subsequently referred the case to the Office of the Chief State's Attorney"
  • "was charged with 42 counts of Possession of Ballots and Envelopes Restricted"
  • "four Bridgeport residents involved in the 2023 Bridgeport Democratic mayoral primary have been criminally charged"
  • "Two Democratic officials in Bridgeport rejected plea offers in court on Tuesday and chose to put their fate into the hands of a jury"
  • "One of seven defendants charged in the Bridgeport mayoral election scandals pleaded guilty to voting fraud charges Wednesday but will avoid jail time under a plea agreement."
  • https://portal.ct.gov/dcj/press-releases/division-of-criminal-justice/02212025bridgeportarrests · https://portal.ct.gov/dcj/press-releases/division-of-criminal-justice/07302025bridgeportarrests · https://ctmirror.org/?p=1178726 · https://ctmirror.org/?p=1130880

179. Bridgeport 2023 as the strongest drop-box case (as held by hand pass C)

UNRESOLVED. In hand pass C, which did not fetch the Bridgeport record and so could not use it. Hand pass B fetched and settled it (entries 172–178); the episode relies on those entries, not on this one.

Case file: Paterson, New Jersey, May 2020 — a voided all-mail council election

Tray: PROVEN as an election voided for procedural violations; the criminal fraud cases collapsed, with no conviction found.

180. It began when postal inspectors found hundreds of ballots bundled in mailboxes

CONFIRMED. The county board disqualified 347 of them.

  • "The investigation started after the U.S. Postal Inspection Service tipped off state law enforcement officials to hundreds of mail-in ballots stuffed in a Paterson mailbox. Other bundled ballots were discovered in another nearby city."
  • "So the Passaic County Board of Elections disqualified the 347 votes"
  • https://www.hawaiipublicradio.org/2020-06-30/n-j-election-fraud-case-draws-a-trump-tweet-but-suggests-safeguards-are-working (NPR, syndicated) · https://news.yahoo.com/347-disqualified-mail-ballots-led-020024630.html

181. About 19% of the city's mail ballots were rejected, and the ward winner led by about 240

CONFIRMED. For the 19% (3,190 ballots). The ward margin is given as 240 by one outlet and 245 by another, so the episode says "about 240". "19% rejected" is not "19% fraud": it is unclear how many were tied to the allegations.

  • "The Passaic County Board of Elections rejected 3,190 ballots, about 19% of the mail-in ballots submitted, for the May 12 election in Paterson, but it is unclear how many were connected to the fraud allegations."
  • "who finished the May 12 election 240 votes ahead"
  • https://www.hawaiipublicradio.org/2020-06-30/n-j-election-fraud-case-draws-a-trump-tweet-but-suggests-safeguards-are-working · https://newjerseyglobe.com/local/amidst-voter-fraud-allegations-judge-orders-new-election-in-paterson/ · https://news.yahoo.com/347-disqualified-mail-ballots-led-020024630.html

182. A Superior Court judge (Aug 19, 2020) set the result aside as "rife with mail in vote procedural violations", leaving fraud to the criminal courts

  • "though he left the decision on whether there was voter fraud to the criminal courts."

183. The court-ordered rerun (Nov 2020) was won by the same challenger, by 14 votes

  • "The true result of the May 12 race, the judge said, was unknowable"

184. Four men were charged in June 2020; as of October 2026 neither councilman has been convicted

CONFIRMED. One councilman's case was dismissed on Apr 13, 2026 on speedy-trial grounds and an undisclosed immunity deal (the state is appealing); the state moved to drop the other in May 2026; the two campaign workers' charges had gone away earlier. None of the charges mention the bundled ballots.

  • "wants to dismiss an indictment against" … "nearly six years after"

185. "The county board decided not to count 800 ballots"

UNRESOLVED. The page it came from (a search summary of an advocacy group's case database) was not opened; the sources opened give 347 disqualified and 3,190 rejected citywide. The 800 is kept off air.

Case file: the scale of proven fraud nationally — real, rare, mostly one person acting alone

Tray: PROVEN: real, rare, mostly one person acting alone, and found by records. NOT CHECKABLE: fraud nobody has detected.

186. Heritage's database lists 1,561 "proven instances" (about 85% convictions) across decades, says it is a sample, and says "nobody really knows the extent"

CORRECTED. (the count is out of date); the caveats are confirmed. The live database (last updated Dec 12, 2025) returns 1,619 results, still about 85% criminal convictions; 1,561 was a November 2024 snapshot. Heritage is an interested source that argues fraud is under-detected, and its count is a floor by its own design. A hand tally of its data file (not Heritage's own figures, so not aired as exact numbers) puts absentee-ballot fraud as the largest category and in-person impersonation as the smallest.

  • "A Sampling of Proven Instances of Election Fraud"
  • "The problem is that nobody really knows the extent of election fraud."
  • "It does not capture all cases and certainly does not capture reported instances or allegations of election fraud, some of which may be meritorious, some not, that are not investigated or prosecuted."
  • "Each and every one of the cases in this database represents an instance in which a public official, usually a prosecutor, thought it serious enough to act upon."
  • https://electionfraud.heritage.org/ · https://electionfraud.heritage.org/about

187. AP's Dec 2021 review of six disputed 2020 states found fewer than 475 potential cases against a combined 311,257-vote margin, nearly all one person acting alone

CONFIRMED. (AP via GPB's reprint). These are potential cases, not convictions, and many were later declined; about 1 in 54,000 of the 25.5 million ballots.

  • "An Associated Press review of every potential case of voter fraud in the six battleground states disputed by" … "has found fewer than 475"
  • "by a combined 311,257 votes out of 25.5 million ballots cast for president. The disputed ballots represent just 0.15% of his victory margin in those states."
  • "Virtually every case was based on an individual acting alone to cast additional ballots."
  • "AP reporters in five states contacted roughly 340 election offices"
  • "prosecutors declined to bring charges in 26 of those cases" (Wisconsin)
  • https://www.gpb.org/news/2021/12/16/far-too-little-vote-fraud-tip-election-trump-ap-finds

188. Brookings: Arizona's Heritage-listed fraud rate is .0000845% of ballots over 25 years, and "no election outcome in the U.S. has ever been altered by ballot fraud"

CORRECTED. The rate is confirmed: 36 cases in 42,626,379 ballots, under one per million. But the same commentary's line that no outcome has ever been altered is contradicted by North Carolina's 9th District (entry 166) and by Heritage's own "election overturned" entries, so the script never borrows it.

  • "in Arizona, Heritage had to go back 25 years, over which there were 36 elections held and 42,626,379 ballots cast, in order to come up with 36 cases of fraud. The percentage of fraudulent votes was a minuscule .0000845%"
  • https://www.brookings.edu/articles/how-widespread-is-election-fraud-in-the-united-states-not-very/

189. The best evidence for "fraud is under-detected": a national estimate of double voting (Goel et al., American Political Science Review, 2020)

CONFIRMED. And it cuts both ways. At most about 1 in 4,000 votes in 2012 were double votes, and a 1.3% clerical error rate could explain all of them; a crude purge rule would block about 300 legitimate votes for each double vote it stopped.

  • "We estimate that at most only 1 in 4,000 votes cast in 2012 were double votes, with measurement error in turnout records possibly explaining a"
  • "a 1.3% clerical error rate would be sufficient to explain all of these apparent double votes"
  • "could impede approximately 300 legitimate votes for each double vote prevented"
  • https://doi.org/10.1017/S000305541900087X

190. Wisconsin's audit bureau (2021): 4 people may have voted twice by absentee ballot in 2020

CONFIRMED. The kind of number the audits turn up: single digits, referred for follow-up. The commission's rebuttal (that most apparent pairs were typos) was not obtained and is not aired.

  • "We analyzed absentee ballot data provided by WEC’s staff and found that these data indicated 4 of the 24 individuals may have voted twice by absentee ballot during the November 2020 General Election."
  • https://legis.wisconsin.gov/lab/media/rz1nj2dh/21-19full.pdf

191. A 2017 Brennan Center critique of Heritage's database (10 in-person impersonation cases, and so on)

UNRESOLVED. In this pass: the document was not obtained. The direction is supported by entry 186 (impersonation is Heritage's smallest category), but Brennan's numbers are not cited on air.

  • The same 2017 report's survey of local officials (about 30 suspected noncitizen votes referred out of 23.5 million) was obtained in a top-up (entry 366); its Heritage critique figures remain unread.

Case file: the 2020 close-out record — what the people in charge said, and what the courts decided

Tray: EXPLAINED for claims of outcome-changing fraud in 2020.

192. The Attorney General at the time, Dec 1, 2020: no fraud on a scale that could have changed the outcome

CONFIRMED. (AP, via a syndicated reprint; the AP's own site refused the fetch). The line is narrower than "no fraud".

  • "To date, we have not seen fraud on a scale that could have affected a different outcome in the election"
  • "U.S. attorneys and FBI agents have been working to follow up specific complaints and information they’ve received, but they’ve uncovered no evidence that would change the outcome of the election."
  • https://cfjctoday.com/2020/12/01/barr-no-evidence-of-fraud-thatd-change-election-outcome/

193. The election infrastructure councils convened by CISA, Nov 12, 2020: "the most secure in American history"

CONFIRMED. The statement came from the government and private-sector councils of election officials and vendors, not from CISA alone, and it is about voting systems, not a finding that no individual committed fraud.

  • "The November 3rd election was the most secure in American history."
  • "There is no evidence that any voting system deleted or lost votes, changed votes, or was in any way compromised."
  • https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/news/joint-statement-elections-infrastructure-government-coordinating-council-election

194. The Supreme Court, Dec 11, 2020 (Texas v. Pennsylvania): denied for lack of standing

CONFIRMED. It was not a ruling on the fraud claims. Two Justices would have let the complaint be filed but would not have granted other relief.

  • "The State of Texas’s motion for leave to file a bill of complaint is denied for lack of standing under Article III of the Constitution."
  • "Texas has not demonstrated a judicially cognizable interest in the manner in which another State conducts its elections."
  • "I would therefore grant the motion to file the bill of complaint but would not grant other relief, and I express no view on any other issue."
  • https://www.supremecourt.gov/orders/courtorders/121120zr_p860.pdf

195. A counted tally of the post-election lawsuits: 64 cases with 187 counts in six states, one minor win ("Lost, Not Stolen", July 2022)

CONFIRMED. Of the 64, 20 were dismissed before a merits hearing, 14 were voluntarily dropped and 30 had a merits hearing. The authors are a group of conservative lawyers and former officials and judges.

  • "filed 64 cases containing 187 counts in the six key battleground states"
  • "For this Report, we examined every count of every case brought in these six battleground states. We include both a narrative for each state and an accompanying Addendum listing each case and its disposition."
  • "twenty were dismissed before a hearing on the merits"
  • "and 30 cases included a hearing on the merits."
  • "Only in one Pennsylvania case involving far too few votes to overturn the results"
  • https://lostnotstolen.org/

Case file: what lawful ballot handling looks like on camera

Tray: None: a reference standard for judging clips, not a claim.

196. Georgia's 2020 drop-box rule: video of every box, two-person sworn collection, a signed transfer form, video kept 30 days

CONFIRMED. As the rule in force on that date.

  • "use motion detection that records one frame, or more, per minute"
  • "Collection of ballots from a drop box must be made by a team of at least two people."
  • "which shall include the date, time, location and number of ballots."
  • "Video recordings of the drop box locations must be retained by the county registrars for 30 days after the final certification of the election"
  • https://saportareport.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/Table-of-Contents-for-SEB-Rule-183-1-14-0.8-.14.pdf

197. Georgia's 2020 absentee-processing rule: scanned ballots go in sealed, labelled containers, in batches of at most 100

APPROXIMATE. The copy read is an advocacy group's reproduction of the rule. And this part covers scanned ballots, while the State Farm Arena carriers held opened, unscanned ballots sealed for the night, which the state's report calls a Fulton process.

  • "A security seal shall be placed on the container."
  • "Absentee ballots shall be processed in batches of not more than 100."
  • "Fulton County Board of Elections and Registration put specific processes in place to store ballot boxes underneath tables" (the state's report of investigation)
  • https://www.acluga.org/sites/default/files/field_documents/acluofgeorgia_letters-to-counties-absentee-ballot-processing-10.19.2020.pdf · https://sos.ga.gov/sites/default/files/2023-06/SEB2020-059%20ROI%20redacted.pdf (read via the Internet Archive)

198. Pennsylvania's guidance: a locked box with a numbered tamper-evident seal, video kept a year, two collectors (one a board employee), and a chain-of-custody form that travels with the ballots

CONFIRMED. And it is guidance ("should"), not statute.

  • "All drop-boxes should be secured by a lock and sealed with a tamper-evident seal that has a unique identifying number."
  • "Video surveillance should be retained securely by the county election office for one year following the deadline to certify the election"
  • "The county board of elections should designate at least two election officials to collect completed ballots from a Ballot Return Site. At least one official should be a Board employee; the other may be a member of law enforcement or other peacekeeping officer."
  • https://www.pa.gov/content/dam/copapwp-pagov/en/dos/resources/voting-and-elections/directives-and-guidance/2025/guidance_absentee-mail%20ballot%20person%20return%20guidance_092225.pdf

199. A Pennsylvania appeals court (Aug 6, 2025, non-precedential) upheld release of county election-building footage with faces blurred

CONFIRMED. (in outline). A viewer can request footage, but it is slow and county by county.

  • "he conceded that the footage could blur or omit people and their faces"
  • https://www.pacourts.us/assets/opinions/Commonwealth/out/1185CD23_8-6-25.pdf?cb=1

Case file: how a viewer can check a clip — the method, and the research on whether people can use it

Tray: None: research on method, not a claim.

200. Fact-checkers ask four questions (is it original, who posted it, where, when) and reverse-search key frames, because no free tool searches whole clips; an upload date is not a film date (attributed to Bellingcat, 2017)

CORRECTED. (attribution). Bellingcat's guide supports the frame reverse-search, the no-whole-clip-search point, the date point and the mirroring trick, but the four-question list is not in it.

  • "there are no freely available tools that allow you to reverse search an entire video clip the same way we can with image files, but we can do the next best thing by reverse image searching thumbnails and screenshots"
  • "there are no indications when the video was filmed, meaning that it could have been from yesterday or five years ago"
  • "Often, people use simple tricks to avoid detection from reverse image searches – mirroring a video, changing the color scheme to black and white, zooming in or out, and so on."
  • https://www.bellingcat.com/resources/how-tos/2017/06/30/advanced-guide-verifying-video-content/

201. The professional checklist is First Draft's five pillars: provenance, source, date, location, motivation

UNRESOLVED. As a citation: the guide was not obtained (the organisation closed in 2022). The episode's checklist cites what was read instead: Bellingcat, InVID, and AFP's stated standard.

  • "We require at least two independent sources of information to verify the central claim of a fact-check." (AFP)
  • https://factcheck.afp.com/how-we-work

202. InVID/WeVerify splits a video into keyframes and reverse-searches them

CONFIRMED. (the tool's documentation; a free browser plugin used by newsrooms).

  • "in order to segment it in keyframes which then can be searched with a right click on Google, Yandex, Tineye and Baidu images"
  • https://www.invid-project.eu/tools-and-services/invid-verification-plugin/

203. Professional fact-checkers read laterally (Wineburg and McGrew, Teachers College Record, 2019)

CONFIRMED. (a preprint copy). A small study of 10 fact-checkers, 10 historians and 25 undergraduates: it shows that experts do it, not that lay people can learn it.

  • "We sampled 45 experienced users of the Internet: 10 Ph.D. historians, 10 professional fact checkers, and 25 Stanford University undergraduates."
  • "In contrast, fact checkers read laterally, leaving a site after a quick scan and opening up new browser tabs in order to judge the credibility of the original site."
  • "Compared to the other groups, fact checkers arrived at more warranted conclusions in a fraction of the time."
  • https://doi.org/10.1177/016146811912101102

204. High-school students taught lateral reading in six lessons (Wineburg et al., Journal of Educational Psychology, 2022)

CONFIRMED. (design and direction): a field study in one district, 271 treatment students against 228 controls. The size is APPROXIMATE: the paper's own effect size was not obtained (paywalled), and Stanford's press release says "twice as likely", from a low base. Say about doubled, from a low base.

  • "students in treatment classrooms (n = 271) were compared to peers (n = 228) in regular classrooms."
  • "were twice as likely to spot questionable websites as they were before the instruction took place"
  • "Even then, however, the students earned only half the total number of possible points."
  • https://mediawell.ssrc.org/citations/lateral-reading-on-the-open-internet/ · https://ed.stanford.edu/news/it-doesn-t-take-long-learn-how-spot-misinformation-online-stanford-study-finds

205. College students in a four-hour online module (Breakstone et al., HKS Misinformation Review, 2021)

CONFIRMED. A before-and-after design with no control group (n = 87).

  • "At pretest, only 3 of 87 students engaged in lateral reading by leaving the original site and consulting at least one other source. At posttest, 67 of 87 did so."
  • "Thirty-six students correctly raised questions about the website’s credibility"
  • https://misinforeview.hks.harvard.edu/article/lateral-reading-college-students-learn-to-critically-evaluate-internet-sources-in-an-online-course/

206. A pop-up reminding adults to read laterally (Panizza et al., Scientific Reports, 2022, 5,387 people)

CONFIRMED. The abstract reads more positively than the overall result: the pop-up raised use of fact-checking strategies, but overall accuracy did not move (78.1% against 78.2% for the control group).

  • "both monetary incentives and pop-up increased the use of fact-checking strategies."
  • "In the control condition, 78.2% of participants correctly guessed the scientific validity of the post (incentive condition: 80.1%; pop-up condition: 78.1%)."
  • https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8981191/

207. Prebunking videos run as YouTube ads (Roozenbeek et al., Science Advances, 2022)

CONFIRMED. With the size stated exactly: in the field study, recognition of a manipulation technique rose about 5% (h = 0.09), narrowly below the authors' own preregistered threshold, measured once within 24 hours. The outcome is recognising a technique in a headline, not spotting a fake video.

  • "We collected a total of 22,632 responses"
  • "we narrowly failed to reach our preregistered smallest effect size of interest (SESOI) of h = 0.10, with an observed effect size of h = 0.09 across all six items."
  • "boosting manipulation technique recognition by about 5% on average"
  • https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9401631/

208. Inoculation has a published critique and a counter-analysis

CONFIRMED. That both exist; the question is still open. A 2023 reanalysis found the gamified versions made people more sceptical of all news; a 2025 meta-analysis, co-authored by two of the original inoculation researchers, found both games and videos improve discrimination without that bias.

  • "Bad News and Go Viral! did not improve discrimination, but rather elicited more “false” responses to all news items (more conservative responding)."
  • "Results show that both gamified and video-based interventions consistently improve discrimination between reliable and unreliable news, without increasing response bias"
  • https://doi.org/10.1037/xge0001395 · https://research-information.bris.ac.uk/en/publications/a-signal-detection-theory-meta-analysis-of-psychological-inoculat/

209. Short "tips to spot false news" (Guess et al., PNAS, 2020)

CONFIRMED. Discernment rose 26.5% in a US sample and 17.5% in a highly educated Indian sample; weeks later the US effect had shrunk by more than half.

  • "the intervention improved discernment between mainstream and false news headlines among both a nationally representative sample in the United States (by 26.5%) and a highly educated online sample in India (by 17.5%)"
  • "While the effect is still present weeks later, its magnitude attenuates by more than half relative to wave 1"
  • https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7355018/

210. Do corrections of election-fraud claims work?

CONFIRMED. The results are mixed. Fact-check tweets did not undo the drop in confidence from fraud claims (2018 experiment); a myth-debunking article after 2020 reduced false beliefs; in 2022 prebunking with substantive security information beat corrections from credible sources; corrections work best on the specific claim they target.

  • "Worryingly, exposure to fact-checks that show these claims to be unfounded does not measurably reduce the damage from these accusations."
  • "The treatment also increased confidence in the 2020 election results."
  • "Correction interventions are most effective at reducing belief in the specific election misinformation targeted by the correction."
  • "Correction interventions are less effective at reducing belief in broad false claims about elections or in bolstering general beliefs in electoral integrity."
  • https://doi.org/10.1017/xps.2021.18 · https://electionlab.mit.edu/sites/default/files/2023-01/carey-fogarty-nyhan-reifler.pdf · https://electionlab.mit.edu/sites/default/files/2024-05/11_fraud-corrections-technical-report.pdf · https://www.democratic-erosion.org/briefs/ (Democratic Erosion evidence brief, Oct 2024)

211. The federal "Rumor vs. Reality" page is gone

CONFIRMED. Fetched on 2026-10-12, the old address returns "not found", and its topic path redirects to the general election-security page, which has no rumour list.

  • https://www.cisa.gov/rumor-vs-reality (returns "not found") · https://www.cisa.gov/topics/election-security/rumor-vs-reality (redirects to https://www.cisa.gov/topics/election-security)

212. What election offices tell voters to do: go to their own state and county offices

CONFIRMED. The secretaries of state's association sends voters to election officials' own sites and to CanIVote.org, and lists state rumour pages. Kentucky's page shows rumours come from every side: its 2020 example was a viral voter-suppression claim against officials. The federal tip sheet for officials has one voter-facing line and no reverse-search method.

  • "#TrustedInfo2026 is NASS's public education effort to promote election officials as the trusted sources of election information."
  • "Can I Vote does not capture any information, but instead links directly to state election websites"
  • "dishonest and irresponsible allegations that Kentucky election officials were engaged in"
  • "Encourage voters to verify the sources of articles, papers, and other resources before sharing them."
  • https://www.nass.org/initiatives/trustedinfo · https://www.nass.org/can-I-vote · https://sos.ky.gov/elections/Pages/Rumor-Control.aspx · https://www.dni.gov/files/FMIC/documents/products/Securing-Election-Infrastructure-Against-The-Tactics-Of-Foreign-Malign-Influence-Operations-Apr2024.pdf

§ 4. Top-up hand passes (2026-10-13, after the host's pitch questions)

After the pitch, the host asked five questions the first research did not answer in full: whether proven fraud gets buried and whether fraud stories "disappear"; why states refuse to give the federal government their voter rolls; where the data are behind "statistically impossible" counts (overnight batches, more than 100% turnout, one-sided batches, Detroit's counting boards); what happened with Pennsylvania's undated mail ballots; and how much noncitizen and other ineligible voting there is. Five more hand passes answered them on 2026-10-13, each tested in both directions, from the primary record wherever it could be fetched. Every quotation relied on below was again checked mechanically against the saved source text. Where a top-up corrects an earlier entry, the earlier entry now points here, and the change is in the Corrections table.

Case file: proven fraud since 2024 — the case sweep

Tray: PROVEN: real cases with their scale, three local elections voided or rerun. OPEN — Maricopa's 2026 noncitizen charges: (a) AP says charges were filed and Votebeat that none were announced; (b) the charging documents would settle it; (c) the Maricopa County Attorney holds them.

There is no national register of election-crime cases. This sweep used the Heritage Foundation's data file, the Justice Department's national releases from Sept 4 to Oct 12, 2026, and state and county prosecutors' releases found by search in thirteen states. It is a wide sample, not a census, and a search miss is a fact about the search.

213. The Justice Department charged at least 57 people with election crimes in six national releases between Sept 4 and Oct 12, 2026, mostly for noncitizen voting

CONFIRMED. (our count: 5 + 16 + 4 + 10 + 6 new + 16, the Minnesota ten counted once). Charged, not convicted. A newspaper's claim of "nearly 50" cases in September against 13 in the whole previous administration was seen only in a search summary (the page refused the fetch) and is UNRESOLVED, not aired.

  • "Today, the Justice Department announced charges against 16 individuals in connection with illegal voting, illegal voter registration, and related election-fraud schemes across the country."
  • "a federal grand jury indicted ten defendants separately on September 24, 2026, for voter fraud"
  • "Today the Justice Department announced charges involving 16 individuals, including an illegal alien, in connection with illegal voting"
  • "A complaint, indictment, and information merely contain accusations."
  • https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/department-justice-charges-16-individuals-illegal-voting-and-related-election-crimes · https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/department-justice-indicts-ten-aliens-voter-fraud-minnesota · https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/justice-department-announces-charges-involving-16-individuals-election-crimes · https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/five-charged-pennsylvania-new-jersey-and-wisconsin-illegally-voting-fraudulent-registration · https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/four-aliens-charged-election-fraud · https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/justice-department-announces-charges-involving-16-aliens-election-crimes

  • "[The county attorney] said she found no evidence that those voters were attempting to sway a specific election."
  • "Though our findings today would not change the outcome of any past election, every ineligible voter dilutes the vote of a citizen"
  • "The remaining 147 voters are still being investigated by her office and the Arizona Attorney General’s Office."

  • "announced the indictments and arrests of six people, including several public officials, for election crimes related to a vote harvesting scheme in Frio County."

  • https://www.ksat.com/news/politics/2025/10/24/7-charged-in-2024-pennsylvania-voter-registration-fraud-that-prosecutors-say-was-motivated-by-money/ · https://whyy.org/articles/pennsylvania-voter-registration-fraud-criminal-charges-2024-election/ · https://www.votebeat.org/pennsylvania/2024/10/25/pennsylvanias-lancaster-county-detects-signs-of-fraud-in-late-arriving-voter-registrations/

225. Harris County, Texas: a court voided the 2022 180th District Court race (about 1,430 illegal votes against a 449-vote margin); the incumbent resigned and dropped his appeal in Sept 2025

CONFIRMED. The illegal votes were mostly residency and ID-paperwork problems plus votes cast in an extra hour the county wrongly allowed, not forged ballots, and the court did not find that anyone intended fraud.

230. A national review (CEIR, via NPR, July 2025): noncitizen voting happens "in minuscule numbers"; 18 states disclosed nothing

CONFIRMED. As CEIR's finding, attributed. CEIR is a nonprofit that argues fraud claims are overstated, and the 18-state gap cuts the other way: absence of disclosure is not absence of cases.

  • "18 states, which span the political spectrum, have not publicly disclosed finding or removing any noncitizen voters on their rolls."

Case file: the peg, updated to 2026-10-13

Tray: OPEN — whether Fulton's repeated scans and missing images were accident or intent: (a) the state found the sloppiness but could not say whether any repeated image was tabulated, or why; (b) the FBI's forensic findings on the 653 seized boxes, or any charging document, would settle it; (c) the FBI and the Justice Department (N.D. Ga.) hold them. Even if every repeated image had been counted, the hand-pass arithmetic puts it at a few hundred net votes against a statewide margin of about 12,000. OPEN — Homeland Security's 42,776 deceased on Georgia's rolls: (a) the method is unpublished and no count of ballots cast in those names exists; (b) the match method and list, and the state's own check, would settle it; (c) Homeland Security and Georgia's Secretary of State hold them. It is about rolls, not ballots.

The script says a second judge quashed a grand-jury subpoena and the government has appealed, and does not resolve the limitations question.

235. Homeland Security's "more than 42,000 deceased registrants" in Georgia has no published method, and a roll entry is not a vote

Case file: do fraud stories "disappear"? Coverage, retractions and walk-backs

Tray: EXPLAINED for proven fraud gets suppressed: every vanished story found was false, though follow-through on convictions is under-covered. OPEN — the Texas Attorney General's removed prosecution tally: (a) removed in 2023 with no stated reason; (b) the office's records on why would settle it; (c) the Texas Attorney General holds them. It counts prosecutions, not votes.

Twelve of the largest or most relevant cases were checked against wire services, national outlets on the left and right, local outlets and the nonpartisan election press. The search tool could not reach Reuters, the New York Times or the New York Post, so those were effectively untested; "not found" means our searches did not surface it.

246. Newspaper attention to voter fraud does not track the number of cases

249. The coverage table: which outlets carried which of twelve cases

APPROXIMATE. As a whole. Each "found" rests on a search hit (fetched or search-only, as marked in the hit list), each "not found" is a search miss, and Reuters, the New York Times and the New York Post were not testable. The pattern: all twelve had local coverage and nine reached AP at some stage; the outcome of a case (a plea, a sentence) got less national attention than the arrest; the gap ran in both political directions; and the nonpartisan election press tracked outcomes most consistently. Don't air no national outlet covered X; air we found it only in local news.

Case file: the states that "refuse to give the government their voter rolls"

Tray: EXPLAINED for states are hiding their rolls: the rolls are public, and three identifiers are withheld under state law. PROVEN: New Jersey's motor-vehicle registration error, and flag lists that proved wrong on checking. OPEN — SAVE's error rate: (a) 28,635+ flags with no published false-positive rate; (b) the resolution of every flag would settle it; (c) Homeland Security/USCIS and the states hold it. OPEN — Homeland Security's 42,776 deceased on Georgia's rolls: (a) the method is unpublished and no count of ballots cast in those names exists; (b) the match method and list, and the state's own check, would settle it; (c) Homeland Security and Georgia's Secretary of State hold them. It is about rolls, not ballots.

250. What the Justice Department demanded: the full list, with date of birth plus a driver's licence number or the last four digits of the Social Security number

CONFIRMED. (the demand letters themselves, signed by the Assistant Attorney General for Civil Rights). At least 48 states and DC were asked; North Dakota has no voter registration, so it was not.

  • "your state’s VRL must include the registrant’s full name, date of birth, residential address, his or her state driver’s license number or the last four digits of the registrant’s social security number as required under the Help America Vote Act" (Oregon, Aug 14, 2025)
  • "within seven days or by August 25, 2025"
  • "At least 48 states and Washington, DC, have received requests for their complete voter registration lists." (Brennan Center tracker)
  • https://www.brennancenter.org/media/14508/download/oregon_08.14.2025_doj-letter.pdf · https://www.democracydocket.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/new-hampshire_08.18.25_doj-letter.pdf · https://www.brennancenter.org/our-work/research-reports/tracker-justice-department-requests-voter-information

251. The statutes cited and the stated purpose: checking the state's list maintenance under federal voter-registration law

CONFIRMED. The letters cite three federal laws (the National Voter Registration Act, the Help America Vote Act and the Civil Rights Act of 1960) and answer privacy objections in advance.

  • "The purpose of the request is to ascertain Oregon’s compliance with the list maintenance requirements of the NVRA and HAVA."
  • "Any statewide prohibitions are clearly preempted by federal law."
  • https://www.brennancenter.org/media/14508/download/oregon_08.14.2025_doj-letter.pdf

252. The purpose given privately, and later in court, was wider: run the rolls against the federal SAVE citizenship check, looking for noncitizens and dead voters

  • "being screened for ineligible voter entries." (a department statement, Sept 2025)

253. The data-sharing agreement states were asked to sign binds them to "clean" their rolls within 45 days of the department's notice, and lets contractors see the data

CONFIRMED. (the executed Texas agreement, Dec 9, 2025). Some complying states pushed back on these terms: Iowa handed over its file but would not sign.

  • "You agree therefore that within forty-five (45) days of receiving that notice from the Justice Department of any issues, insufficiencies, inadequacies, deficiencies, anomalies, or concerns, your state will clean its VRL/Data by removing ineligible voters and resubmit the updated VRL/Data"
  • "a contractor with the Department of Justice who needs access to the VRL/Data information in order to perform duties related to the Department's list maintenance verification procedures."
  • "there are potential conflicts between the terms of the MOU and the National Voter Registration Act of 1993" (Iowa)

On air: the department said it wasn't building a national list; a later executive order told Homeland Security to compile a State Citizenship List. Don't say a national database exists.

256. The state-by-state record: about 16 states gave the full file; 3 gave only the public file and were not sued; 30 states and DC were sued, 26 dismissed, Oklahoma settled, 4 pending

CONFIRMED. (status per the trackers, current to Oct 8–13, 2026). Full file: Wyoming, Indiana, Alaska, Texas, Mississippi, Alabama, Arkansas, Louisiana, Tennessee, South Dakota, Florida, Nebraska, Ohio, Iowa, Oklahoma (after a settlement) and South Carolina. Public file only, not sued: Kansas (disputed: the department told a court Kansas gave the full list; Kansas says only the public version), Missouri and Montana.
